About this text

The Compendium of the Reality of All Tathāgatas, known in Sanskrit as the Sarvatathāgatatattvasaṃgraha and in Tibetan as De bzhin gshegs pa thams cad kyi de kho na nyid bsdus pa, is a foundational scripture of Buddhist yoga tantra that presents itself as a Great Vehicle sūtra. Set in the Akaniṣṭha heaven, it describes how the Blessed One Vairocana, endowed with the blessing of all the tathāgatas, reveals the great Vajradhātu maṇḍala and its families of buddhas and bodhisattvas, together with their mantras, mudrās and rites of consecration, and it recounts the awakening of the Buddha through the meditative process of realizing oneself as a buddha. The work also tells of the subjugation of Maheśvara and the worldly gods by Vajrapāṇi in his wrathful form as Trailokyavijaya, and it closes with a hymn of one hundred and eight names in praise of Vajrapāṇi as Vajrasattva. It is Toh 479 in the Kangyur, where it belongs to the Tantra section among the yoga tantras, and it is traditionally said to have been translated into Tibetan by the Indian scholar Śraddhākaravarman and the Tibetan translator Rinchen Zangpo.

Translation

In the language of India: sarbbatathāgatatattvasaṃgrahanāmamahāyānasūtra

In the language of Tibet: The Great Vehicle Sūtra Called the Compendium of the Reality of All Tathāgatas.

The first fascicle.

Homage to all the buddhas and bodhisattvas.

Thus did I hear at one time. The Blessed One Vairocana, of great compassion, was dwelling in the abode of the king of the gods of Akaniṣṭha. He was endowed with the vajra blessing of all the tathāgatas and with the distinctions of the manifold kinds of samaya wisdom. He had attained the empowerment of Dharma king of the three realms through the jeweled crown of all the tathāgatas. He was the lord of the great yoga of the omniscient wisdom of all the tathāgatas. He had realized all the mudrās of all the tathāgatas as sameness, and, by accomplishing the purposes of all, he performed the activity of fulfilling the hopes of all the realms of sentient beings, without remainder and without exception. Vairocana of great compassion, abiding forever in the three times, was the Tathāgata whose body, speech and mind had all become like vajra. That abode, where all the tathāgatas dwell and which they praise, was adorned with gems and great jewels, many colored and hung with bells, and made beautiful with silks, banners, garlands of flowers, yak-tail whisks, nets and half-nets, and moon ornaments stirred by the wind.

He was there together with ninety-nine koṭis of bodhisattvas, namely the bodhisattva great beings Vajrapāṇi, Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord, Ākāśagarbha, Vajramuṣṭi, Mañjuśrī, Sahacittotpādadharmacakrapravartin, Gaganagañja, Sarvamārabalapramardin and others, ninety-nine koṭis of bodhisattva great beings, and with tathāgatas as numerous as the sands of the river Ganges. Moreover, Jambudvīpa appeared to be filled with the bodies of those immeasurable, countless tathāgatas, like a pod filled with sesame seeds. From the body of each of the tathāgatas, too, countless, immeasurable buddha fields appeared. And in those buddha fields, these modes of the Dharma were being taught.

Then the great Blessed One Vairocana abided forever like the entire sphere of space, his body, speech and mind having become like vajra. By entering into sameness with all the tathāgatas, he was the wisdom being who realizes the entire vajra sphere; the essence of wisdom arisen from the vajra blessing of the most subtle atoms of the entire sphere of space; the great jewel of the empowerment with the jewel of the great vajra wisdom of the infinity of the tathāgatas. He became awakened through manifest and perfect awakening to the wisdom of suchness that extends through the entire sphere of space. Because the essential nature of all the tathāgatas is pure, he realized that all phenomena are pure by nature. He truly displayed all forms pervading the entire element of space. Through his knowledge he became supreme in the conduct of taming the realms of sentient beings, without remainder and without exception. By receiving the command of all the tathāgatas and making it bear fruit, he performed all activities, unequaled and unsurpassed. He was all of these: the mind steadfast in the great awakening of all the tathāgatas; the samaya of summoning all the tathāgatas; the lord of wisdom who delights all the tathāgatas; the one to whom all the tathāgatas say, "Excellent!"; the conferring of the great jewel empowerment of all the tathāgatas; the maṇḍala of the sunlight of all the tathāgatas; the jewel splendor of the king of wish-fulfilling gems, king of the aspirations of all the tathāgatas; the great laughter of all the tathāgatas; the great pure Dharma of all the tathāgatas; the wisdom of the great discernment of all the tathāgatas; the wheel of all the tathāgatas; the explaining of the intention of all the tathāgatas; the unfailing fulfilling of the intentions of all, of all the tathāgatas; the utterly firm armor of the great diligence of all the tathāgatas; the yakṣa who guards and protects for all the tathāgatas; and the wisdoms that bind the vajra mudrā of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas.

Samantabhadra, wholly unfailing,
Māra, and the Lord of Supreme Delight,
The Sky-Born, of very great splendor,
Jewel Glory and Great Smile,
The great Lord of the All-Seeing Gaze,
Mañjuśrī, and All the Maṇḍalas,
The Speechless One, and the One of All Actions,
Fierce Diligence, and the One Who Holds Firm;
Vajra hook, bow and arrow,
Jewel, sun, banner, smile,
Lotus, sword, wheel, speech,
Action, armor, the terrifier, binding;
Without beginning or end, and peaceful,
Fierce, wrathful, of great patience,
Yakṣa, rākṣasa, holy, steadfast,
Of great splendor, hero, great lord,
The Lord of Umā, protector of creatures,
Desiring Inclusion, Desiring Victory, the great sage,
World protectors, and sky and earth,
The three worlds and the three realms,
The great elements, who greatly accomplish the aims of beings,
All, Śiva and Brahmā,
Saṃsāra and nirvāṇa and permanence,
Right livelihood and the great offering,
Buddha, purity, the Great Vehicle:
The three existences and permanence are also that.

Victor over the Three Worlds, and Blissful Conduct,
Lord of Blissful Conduct, the One Who Thoroughly Tames,
Vajra Lord, and the supreme ground,
The way of the perfection of wisdom,
The liberation of the bodhisattvas,
The conduct of all the tathāgatas,
The aim of the buddhas and the mind of the buddhas,
All awakening and the unsurpassed,
Vairocana, chief of the victors,
The self-arisen dhāraṇī and mindfulness,
The great being and the great mudrā,
Samādhi, performing the activity of the buddhas,
The perfect Buddha, the nature of all,
The one who realizes existence, permanence and the meaning,
The great trunk, the Great Black One,
Great passion, great bliss,
Great means, and the great supreme of the supreme,
Supreme of all, and lord of abodes,
The Blessed One, the mind of great awakening.

The bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra dwells in the heart of all the tathāgatas.

Then all the tathāgatas filled this buddha field completely, like a pod full of sesame seeds. Then all the tathāgatas gathered and went to where the great bodhisattva called Sarvārthasiddhi was seated at the seat of awakening. Displaying their bodies of perfect enjoyment especially for that bodhisattva, they spoke as follows: "Son of good family, why is it that, without having realized the reality of all the tathāgatas, you apply yourself with zeal to all these austerities, thinking, 'How shall I manifestly and perfectly awaken to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening?'"

Then the bodhisattva great being Sarvārthasiddhi, roused by all the tathāgatas, arose from that unmoving samādhi, bowed to all the tathāgatas, and said: "Blessed Ones, Tathāgatas, what is the reality of the tathāgatas like? Please tell me how I should practice."

When he had made this request, all those tathāgatas spoke with a single voice: "Son of good family, examine your own mind and settle in equipoise, and practice by reciting as much as you wish this naturally accomplished mantra: oṃ cittaprativedhaṃ karomi."

Then the bodhisattva Sarvārthasiddhi said to all the tathāgatas: "Blessed Ones, Tathāgatas, I perceive it to be like a moon disk in my heart."

All the tathāgatas declared: "Son of good family, that mind is luminous by nature, and so it becomes just as it is cultivated, like a white cloth that is dyed with color."

Then, in order to increase his knowledge of the naturally luminous mind, all the tathāgatas, through this naturally accomplished mantra, oṃ bodhicitta utpādayāmi, caused that great bodhisattva's mind to be generated once more.

Then that bodhisattva, having generated the awakening mind at the command of all the tathāgatas, said: "What I saw as like a moon disk, I now see as the very moon disk itself."

All the tathāgatas declared: "You have made manifest the generation of the mind that is the essence of all the tathāgatas, Samantabhadra. Accomplish it well. In order to make firm that generation of the mind of all the tathāgatas, Samantabhadra, imagine with this mantra the form of a vajra on the moon disk in your own heart: oṃ tiṣṭha vajra."

The bodhisattva said: "Blessed Ones, Tathāgatas, I see a vajra resting upon the moon disk."

All the tathāgatas declared: "Make firm, with this mantra, that vajra, the Samantabhadra mind of all the tathāgatas: oṃ vajra atmako'haṃ."

Then, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, all the vajra spheres of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, as many as there are, entering into sameness throughout the entire sphere of space, entered into that sattva vajra. Then all the tathāgatas empowered the Blessed One, the great bodhisattva Sarvārthasiddhi, with the name "Vajradhātu, Vajradhātu."

Then the bodhisattva Vajradhātu said to all those tathāgatas: "Blessed Ones, Tathāgatas, I clearly see the body of all the tathāgatas."

All the tathāgatas declared: "Therefore, great being, meditate on yourself, that sattva vajra, as the body of a buddha endowed with all the supreme aspects, reciting as much as you wish the naturally accomplished mantra: oṃ yathā sarbbatathāgatastathā ahaṃ."

When this had been declared, the great bodhisattva Vajradhātu, having manifestly and perfectly awakened as a tathāgata himself, bowed to all the tathāgatas and said: "Blessed Ones, all you tathāgatas, bless me and make this manifest and perfect awakening firm."

When he had made this request, all the tathāgatas entered into that sattva vajra of the Tathāgata Vajradhātu. Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Vajradhātu, at that very instant, manifestly and perfectly awakened to the wisdom of the sameness of all the tathāgatas. He entered the secret samaya of the mudrā of the wisdom of the vajra sameness of all the tathāgatas. He entered the secret samaya of the mudrā of the wisdom of the sameness of the vajra jewel empowerment of all the tathāgatas. Realizing the wisdom of the sameness of the Dharma of all the tathāgatas, he was pure by nature. He became a tathāgata, an arhat, a perfect and complete buddha, the source of wisdom, naturally luminous in the sameness of all of all the tathāgatas.

Then again all the tathāgatas came forth from that sattva vajra of all the tathāgatas. They generated in him the wisdom of the sameness of all the tathāgatas, and empowered him with the empowerment of the great precious gem that arises from space. They generated in him the Dharma wisdom of Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord, and set him over all the activities of all the tathāgatas. Then they went to the summit of Mount Meru, to the peaked pavilion whose pinnacle is made of vajra gems and jewels. There the Tathāgata Vajradhātu, blessed as all the tathāgatas, became inseparable from them, and sat upon the lion throne of all the tathāgatas, his faces turned in every direction.

Then the Tathāgata Akṣobhya, the Tathāgata Ratnasambhava, the Tathāgata Avalokiteśvararāja and the Tathāgata Amoghasiddhi, each of them blessing himself as all the tathāgatas, sat in the four directions, regarding all directions as the same, because the Blessed One Śākyamuni had fully realized the sameness of all the tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, not long after the Tathāgata had manifestly and perfectly awakened, was the mind of all the tathāgatas called Samantabhadra; he had been empowered with the empowerment of the great precious gem that arises from space of all the tathāgatas; he had attained the perfection of the sublime Dharma wisdom of Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord of all the tathāgatas; he unfailingly displayed all the activities of all the tathāgatas, unobstructed, his intentions accomplished, and every aspiration of his mind entirely fulfilled. Blessing himself as all the tathāgatas, he entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of the sattva, arisen from the samaya of the great bodhisattva of all the tathāgatas called Samantabhadra. And from his own heart he drew forth this essence of all the tathāgatas called the realization of the Great Vehicle of all the tathāgatas: vajrasatva.

Then, as soon as this had come forth from the heart of all the tathāgatas, that very Blessed One Samantabhadra came forth, having become moon disks. Thoroughly purifying the great awakening mind of all sentient beings, they settled in the various directions of all the tathāgatas. Then from those moon disks, in turn, came forth wisdom vajras of all the tathāgatas, which entered into the heart of the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Vairocana. Because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is utterly firm, and through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, they blended into one. Pervading the entire element of space with garlands of five-pointed rays of light, they became the vajra-like sphere of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas; they came forth from the heart of all the tathāgatas and rested in his two hands.

Then from that vajra came forth vajra-like rays of light of various forms and colors, filling all the world systems with radiance. From the tips of those vajra-like rays came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. Throughout the whole all-pervading dharmadhātu, throughout all that reaches to the limits of the element of space, and in all the clouds of the vast oceans of world systems, they displayed, through manifest awakening to the wisdom of the sameness of all the tathāgatas, the generation of the great awakening mind of all the tathāgatas, the accomplishment of the manifold conduct of Samantabhadra, the pleasing of the families of all the tathāgatas, the going to the great seat of awakening, the taming of all the māras, the manifest awakening to the great awakening of the sameness of all the tathāgatas, and the turning of the wheel of the holy Dharma, up to the protecting of all the realms of sentient beings, without remainder and without exception, as long as they last, the bringing of benefit and happiness to all, and the accomplishment of the supreme siddhi of the wisdom and superknowledges of all the tathāgatas, and so on, all the miraculous powers and magical displays of all the tathāgatas. Then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is Samantabhadra and because it is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the great bodhisattva Samantabhadra. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! I am the self-arisen
Samantabhadra, the steadfast being.
Because of that steadfastness, though bodiless,
I have taken on the body of a being.

Then the body of the great bodhisattva Samantabhadra came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk before all the tathāgatas, remained there awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the commitment (samaya) of the samādhi and wisdom of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely, the moral discipline, samādhi, wisdom, liberation, and vision of the wisdom of liberation of all the tathāgatas; the turning of the wheel of Dharma; the accomplishment of the aims of sentient beings; great skillful means; power; diligence; the commitment of great wisdom; the complete protection of the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder; the bringing of all to sovereignty; and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the attainment of the supreme accomplishment of the equality wisdom of all the tathāgatas, the unsurpassed higher knowledges, and full realization of the Great Vehicle, he empowered that bodhisattva, the great being Samantabhadra, as the universal monarch of all the tathāgatas, with the empowerment of the jeweled crown ornament and diadem of the body of all the buddhas, and placed in his hand that vajra of the accomplishment of all the tathāgatas.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajrapāṇi, Vajrapāṇi."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, rested his left hand proudly with the vajra, and with his right hand held the vajra at his heart as though brandishing and about to hurl it, and he uttered this inspired utterance:

This unsurpassed vajra
Of the accomplishment of all the buddhas
Has been given to me, into my own hand:
Vajra is established upon vajra.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of the being arisen from the commitment of the great bodhisattva Amogharāja, and from his own heart he drew forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of inviting all the tathāgatas:

vajrarāj

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajrapāṇi. Becoming the great hook of all the tathāgatas, it came forth and entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where it blended into one and took the form of a great vajra hook, resting in the two hands of the Blessed One. Then from that great form of the vajra hook there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed the inviting of all the tathāgatas and the other magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is the supremely unfailing king and because it is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the great bodhisattva Amogha. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! I am the hook arisen from vajra,
The king who never fails.
Since the buddhas pervade everywhere,
To accomplish their aims I invite them from everywhere.

Then the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Amogharāja, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk to the right of all the tathāgatas, remained there awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the commitment of inviting all the tathāgatas. In order to accomplish the commitment of inviting all the tathāgatas, the summoning of all the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the sublime blessing of the gathering of all the tathāgatas, he likewise placed that vajra hook in the hand of the bodhisattva Amogharāja.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajrākarṣa, Vajrākarṣa."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrākarṣa, invited all the tathāgatas with that vajra hook and uttered this inspired utterance:

This unsurpassed wisdom
Of the vajra of all the buddhas
Is the sublime invitation from everywhere,
To accomplish the aims of all the buddhas.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra blessed by the being arisen from the commitment of the great bodhisattva called Māra, and from his own heart came forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of delighting all the tathāgatas:

vajrarāga

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajradhara. Becoming the flower arrows of all the tathāgatas, it came forth and entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where it blended into one and took the form of a great vajra arrow, resting in his two hands. Then from that form of the vajra arrow there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed the delighting of all the tathāgatas and the other magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is supremely the work of Māra and because it is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the great bodhisattva Māra. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! I am pure by intrinsic nature,
The one who delights the self-arisen ones.
In order to purify those free of attachment,
I tame them by means of attachment.

Then the body of that great bodhisattva Māra came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk to the left of all the tathāgatas, remained there, again awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra blessed with the delighting of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely, the vajra commitment of Māra of all the tathāgatas, the gratifying of the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the attainment of the supreme accomplishment of Māra of all the tathāgatas, he likewise placed that vajra arrow in the hand of the bodhisattva, the great being called Māra.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajradhanu, Vajradhanu."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being called Vajradhanu, with that vajra arrow performed the work of Māra upon all the tathāgatas, and uttered this inspired utterance:

This stainless wisdom
Of the attachment of all the buddhas
Slays non-attachment by means of attachment
And bestows every bliss.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of the being arisen from the commitment of the bodhisattva, the great being Prāmodyarāja, and from his own heart came forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of the utter joy of all the tathāgatas:

vajrasādhu

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajradhara. Becoming the cry "Excellent!", it entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where it blended into one and took the form of vajra delight, resting in his two hands. Then from that form of vajra delight there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed the crying of "Excellent!" by all the tathāgatas and the other magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is that which brings supreme joy and because it is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Prāmodyarāja. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! Since I am supreme
Among all the omniscient ones' gifts of "Excellent!",
I am the one who surely brings forth delight
In those who are free of conceptual thought.

Then the body of the great bodhisattva Prāmodyarāja came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk behind all the tathāgatas, remained there, again awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of delighting all the tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely, the commitment of the unsurpassed wisdom of the utter delight of all the tathāgatas, the gratifying of all sentient beings in the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the attainment of the sublime accomplishment of the unsurpassed taste of the delight of all the tathāgatas, he likewise placed that vajra delight in the hand of the great bodhisattva Prāmodyarāja.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajraharṣa, Vajraharṣa."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajraharṣa, with that form of vajra delight, brought utter delight to all the tathāgatas with the cry "Excellent!", and uttered this inspired utterance:

This giving of "Excellent!" by all the buddhas,
Because it sets all things in motion,
Delights all the vajra ones
And increases divine delight.

The great awakening mind, the commitment of inviting all the tathāgatas, the wisdom of delighting all the tathāgatas, and great delight: these four are the beings of the great commitment of all the tathāgatas.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of the jewel, arisen from the commitment of the bodhisattva, the great being Ākāśagarbha, and from his own heart he drew forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of empowerment by all the tathāgatas:

vajraratna

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is the thorough realization of the wisdom of the equality of all things with space, this was that same Blessed One Vajradhara, and it came forth as light rays filling all of space. Those light rays of all of space illuminated all the world systems, so that all became like the expanse of space. Then, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, all those expanses of space entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, and because the samādhi of Vajrasattva had been thoroughly cultivated, they took the form of a great vajra jewel, its size equal to all the world systems arisen from the whole element of space, and it rested in the two hands of the Tathāgata. Then from that form of the vajra jewel there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the fine atoms of all the world systems. They performed the empowerment of all the tathāgatas and the other magical feats and miraculous displays of all the tathāgatas throughout all the world systems, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva has arisen fully from the essence of the whole element of space and because it is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Ākāśagarbha. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! Since even the Victors, free of attachment,
Regard me as lord of the three realms,
I am the supreme empowerment
Of the sublime vajra jewel.

Then the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Ākāśagarbha, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk before all the tathāgatas, remained there, again awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the precious jewel of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of the commitment of fulfilling the intentions of all the tathāgatas, the complete accomplishment of all the aims of the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the attainment of the accomplishment of the ultimate excellence of all the tathāgatas, he empowered the bodhisattva, the great being Ākāśagarbha, to turn the wheel of the vajra jewel, with the empowerment of the sprout of the vajra jewel, and placed that vajra gem in his hand.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajragarbha, Vajragarbha."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajragarbha, set that vajra gem in the place of his own empowerment, and uttered this inspired utterance:

I am that empowerment,
The form of the being of all the buddhas;
Since it has been given into my hand,
Jewel is placed upon jewel.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of the jewel, arisen from the commitment of the bodhisattva, the great being called Mahātejas, and from his own heart he drew forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of the light rays of all the tathāgatas:

vajrateja

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajrapāṇi. Becoming a great sun disk, it entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where it blended into one and took the form of a vajra sun, resting in the two hands of the Blessed One. Then from that vajra sun disk there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed the radiating of light rays by all the tathāgatas and the other magical feats and miraculous displays of all the tathāgatas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is supremely brilliant and utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Mahātejas. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! I am the incomparable brilliance
That illumines all the realms of beings,
For I bring together
Even the guides, the pure buddhas.

Then the body of that bodhisattva, the great being of stainless brilliance, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk to the right of all the tathāgatas, remained there awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of the disk of light of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely, the commitment of the light rays of all the tathāgatas, the unequaled brilliance of the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the attainment of the light of all the tathāgatas and the sublime accomplishment, he likewise placed that vajra sun in the hand of the bodhisattva Mahātejas.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajratejas, Vajratejas."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being, with that vajra sun illumined all the tathāgatas, and uttered this inspired utterance:

This illumination of all the buddhas,
Which destroys the black darkness of ignorance,
Far surpasses the light of suns
As numerous as the finest atoms.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of the jewel, arisen from the commitment of the bodhisattva, the great being Ratnaśrī, and from his own heart he drew forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of fulfilling the intentions of all the tathāgatas:

vajraketu

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajradhara. Becoming victory banners of various colors, forms, ornaments and shapes, it came forth and entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where it blended into one and took the form of a vajra banner, resting in his two hands. Then from that form of the vajra banner there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed the raising of the jewel banners of all the tathāgatas and the other magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is the glory of the jewel and is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the bodhisattva Ratnaśrī. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! I am the incomparable glory
That accomplishes every aim,
Fulfills every intention,
And brings all purposes to completion.

Then the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Ratnaśrī, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk to the left of all the tathāgatas, remained there, again awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra blessed by the banner of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely, the commitment of raising the banner of the wish-fulfilling king of gems of all the tathāgatas, the complete fulfillment of all the hopes of the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the attainment of the sublime accomplishment of the great aim of all the tathāgatas, he likewise placed that vajra banner in the hand of the great bodhisattva Ratnaśrī.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajraketu, Vajraketu."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajraketu, with that vajra banner set all the tathāgatas to the practice of the perfection of generosity, and uttered this inspired utterance:

The banner of the wish-fulfilling gem,
Which brings to complete fulfillment
All the intentions of all the buddhas,
Is the way of the perfection of generosity.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of the jewel, arisen from the commitment of the bodhisattva, the great being Nityaprītipramuditendriya, and from his own heart he drew forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of the delight of all the tathāgatas:

vajrahās

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajradhara. Becoming the smiles of all the tathāgatas, it came forth and entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where it blended into one and took the form of a vajra smile, resting in his two hands. Then from that form of the vajra smile there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed the marvels of all the tathāgatas and the other magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is ever joyful, with faculties utterly delighted, and is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Nityaprītipramuditendriya. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

Because those who are always in meditative equipoise
Devote themselves fully to the aims of the buddhas,
How wonderful! I am the great and marvelous laughter
Of those who are supreme among all.

Then the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Nityaprītipramuditendriya, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk behind all the tathāgatas, remained there, again awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra blessed by the marvels of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely, the commitment of producing the marvels of all the tathāgatas, the making unsurpassed of the faculties of all the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the full attainment of the wisdom that purifies the faculties of all the tathāgatas, he likewise placed that vajra smile in the hand of the great bodhisattva Nityaprītipramuditendriya.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajrahāsa, Vajrahāsa."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrahāsa, with that vajra smile brought delight to all the tathāgatas, and uttered this inspired utterance:

This, which reveals and brings forth
The marvels of all the buddhas,
Is the wisdom that brings great delight,
Which other systems of teaching cannot know.

Great empowerment, the disk of light a fathom wide, the aim of the great being, and great delight: these four are the beings of the great empowerment of all the tathāgatas.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of Dharma, arisen from the commitment of the bodhisattva, the great being Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord, and from his own heart he drew forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of the Dharma of all the tathāgatas:

vajradharmma

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajradhara. Because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is the full realization of the wisdom of the equality of phenomena, pure by their intrinsic nature, it came forth as light rays of the sublime Dharma. Those light rays of the sublime Dharma illumined all the world systems everywhere, so that they became like the expanse of phenomena. And all those expanses of phenomena entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where they blended into one and took the form of a great vajra lotus, its size equal to the whole of space, resting in his two hands. Then from that form of the vajra lotus there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They filled all the world systems with the samādhi, wisdom, higher knowledges and so on of all the tathāgatas, and with the magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord and is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the great bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! I am the ultimate truth,
Self-arisen and pure from the very beginning,
For through phenomena that are like a raft
Complete purity comes to be perceived.

Then the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana and, resting on a moon disk before all the tathāgatas, remained there awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra arisen from the commitment of the wisdom of the equality of the samādhi of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely, the commitment of the complete purification of all the tathāgatas, the complete purification of self and others among the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to bringing about the attainment of the Dharma wisdom and the six higher knowledges of all the tathāgatas, he empowered the great bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord to turn the wheel of the sublime Dharma, with the empowerment of the Dharma body of all the tathāgatas, and placed that vajra lotus in his hand.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajranetra, Vajranetra."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajranetra, opened the petals of that vajra lotus and gazed with a gaze that sees attachment as completely pure, as having the essential nature of non-attachment, and he uttered this inspired utterance:

This realization of the very reality
Of the attachment of all the buddhas
Has been given to me, into my own hand:
Dharma will abide in Dharma.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of Dharma, arisen from the commitment of the bodhisattva, the great being Mañjuśrī, and from his own heart he drew forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of the discernment and great wisdom of all the tathāgatas:

vajratīkṣṇa

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajradhara. It became swords of discernment and wisdom and, coming forth, entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where it blended into one and took the form of a vajra sword, resting in the two hands of the Blessed One. Then from that form of the vajra sword there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed the discernment and wisdom of all the tathāgatas and so on, and the magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is supremely gentle glory and is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the bodhisattva, the great being, the youthful Mañjuśrī. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! I am held to be
The Gentle Voice of all the buddhas,
For although discernment has no form,
It is perceived as voice itself.

Then the body of the bodhisattva, the great being Mañjuśrī, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One and, resting on a moon disk to the right of all the tathāgatas, remained there, again awaiting their command.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra arisen from the commitment of the discernment and wisdom of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely, the commitment of cutting off all the afflictions of all the tathāgatas, the cutting off of all the suffering of the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and the causing of all to experience bliss and happiness of mind, up to the complete perfection of the sublime discernment that follows the speech of all the tathāgatas, he likewise placed that vajra sword in the hand of the bodhisattva, the great being Mañjuśrī.

Then all the tathāgatas empowered him with the empowerment of a vajra name, calling him "Vajrabuddhi, Vajrabuddhi."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrabuddhi, wielded that vajra sword toward all the tathāgatas, and uttered this inspired utterance:

This is the way of the perfection of discernment
Of all the buddhas,
Supreme in cutting down all enemies
And in vanquishing all evil.

Then once again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the vajra of the blessing of Dharma, arisen from the commitment of the bodhisattva, the great being Sahacittotpādadharmacakrapravartin, and from his own heart he drew forth this heart essence of all the tathāgatas, called the commitment of the wheel of all the tathāgatas:

vajrahetu

Then, the very moment it came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that same Blessed One Vajradhara. It became the maṇḍalas of all the tathāgatas, beginning with the great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu, and, coming forth, entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, where it blended into one and took the form of a vajra wheel, resting in the two hands of the Blessed One. Then from that form of the vajra wheel there came forth bodies of all the tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed throughout all the world systems the turning of the wheel of Dharma as soon as the mind is raised, and the other magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is the turning of the wheel of Dharma as soon as the mind is raised and is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the bodhisattva, the great being called Sahacittotpādadharmacakrapravartin. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

"Of those who possess the supreme vajra Dharma,
How wonderful, I am the vajra wheel!
For by merely raising the mind
The wheel of Dharma comes to be turned."

Then that body of the bodhisattva, the great being Sahacittotpādadharmacakrapravartin, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One, took its place on a moon maṇḍala to the left of all the tathāgatas, and again sat awaiting his command.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Wheel Vajra of All Tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely the pledge of the great maṇḍala of all the tathāgatas, the irreversible wheel by which the realm of sentient beings enters, entirely and without remainder, the experience of every happiness and mental joy, and so on up to the supreme accomplishment of the turning of the wheel of the holy Dharma by all the tathāgatas, he placed that vajra wheel in the same way into the hands of that great bodhisattva Sahacittotpādadharmacakrapravartin. Then all the tathāgatas consecrated him with the vajra-name consecration, "Vajragarbha, Vajragarbha." Then the bodhisattva, the great being, establishing the tathāgatas in irreversibility with that vajra wheel, uttered this inspired utterance:

"This is, for all the buddhas,
What purifies all dharmas entirely;
And the irreversible wheel
Is held to be the seat of awakening."

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Blessed by the Dharma Arisen from the Pledge of the Great Bodhisattva the Speechless One, and drew forth from his own heart this essence of all the tathāgatas, called the Pledge of the Speech of All Tathāgatas:

vajrabhāṣ

Then, as soon as it had come forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that very Blessed One Vajrapāṇi. Becoming the syllables of the nature of reality of all the tathāgatas, it came forth, entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, and blended into one. Then it became the form of vajra speech and rested in his two hands. Then from that form of vajra speech there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the fine atoms of all the world systems. They performed the nature of reality of all the tathāgatas and so on, and the magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas and so on, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is utterly beyond words and utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the great bodhisattva the Speechless One. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the secret of the self-born ones,
Held to be intentional speech,
For the teaching of the holy Dharma
Is utterly free from verbal proliferation."

Then that body of the great bodhisattva the Speechless One came forth from the heart of the Blessed One, took its place on a moon maṇḍala behind all the tathāgatas, and sat awaiting his command.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi arisen from the vajra pledge of the speech of the Speechless One of all the tathāgatas. For the sake of the pledge of the wisdom of the speech of all the tathāgatas, the accomplishment of the speech of the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, the experience of every happiness and mental joy, and so on up to the supreme accomplishment of attaining the secret of the speech of all the tathāgatas, he placed that vajra speech in the same way into the hands of that bodhisattva, the great being the Speechless One. Then all the tathāgatas consecrated him with the vajra-name consecration, "Vajrabhāṣa, Vajrabhāṣa." Then the great bodhisattva Vajrabhāṣa, speaking rightly to all the tathāgatas with vajra speech, uttered this inspired utterance:

"This, for all the buddhas,
Is declared to be vajra recitation;
The mantras of all the tathāgatas
Will swiftly be accomplished."

The wisdom of the vajra nature of reality, the wisdom of the discernment of all the tathāgatas, the wisdom of turning the great wheel of Dharma, and the wisdom that turns back the verbal proliferation of all the tathāgatas: these four are the wisdom beings of all the tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Blessed by Action, arisen from the pledge called All the Activities of the Great Bodhisattvas of All Tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this essence of all the tathāgatas, called the Pledge of the Activity of All Tathāgatas:

vajra karma

Then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva thoroughly realizes the wisdom of the sameness of all actions, as soon as it had come forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. Becoming rays of light of the activity of all the tathāgatas, it came forth, and with those rays of light of the activity of all the tathāgatas it illuminated all the world systems and became like the form of the activity of all the tathāgatas. Then that form of the activity of all the tathāgatas also entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana and became of a size that pervades evenly the whole expanse of space. From that form of the activity of all the tathāgatas, it became the form of the vajra of all actions and rested in his two hands.

Then again from that vajra form there came forth the bodies of all the tathāgatas, as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed throughout all the world systems the activities of all the tathāgatas and the rest, and the magical feats and miraculous displays of the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is the boundless activity of all the tathāgatas and is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the great bodhisattva called All the Activities of All the Tathāgatas. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! Of the buddhas, truly,
I am all the many activities;
Because the aim of the buddhas is spontaneously accomplished,
The vajra activity comes into play."

Then that body of the great bodhisattva called All the Activities of All the Tathāgatas came forth from the heart of the Blessed One, took its place on a moon maṇḍala in front of all the tathāgatas, and again sat awaiting his command.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Blessed by the Vajra Arisen from the Unfailing Pledge of All Tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely the pledge of all the unfailing, immeasurable and extensive ritual actions, such as making offerings to all the tathāgatas, the accomplishment of all actions by the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, the experience of every happiness and mental joy, and so on up to the accomplishment of the vajra activity, the wisdom and the supreme superknowledges of all the tathāgatas, he consecrated the great bodhisattva, with the vajra consecration of all the tathāgatas, as the universal monarch of all the activities of all the tathāgatas, and placed that action vajra in the same way into his hands. Then all the tathāgatas consecrated him with the name consecration, "Vajraviśva, Vajraviśva." Then the great bodhisattva called Vajraviśva placed that vajra at his own heart and, applying himself to the activity of all the tathāgatas, uttered this inspired utterance:

"This, for all the buddhas,
Is the supreme performer of every action;
It is I, given into my own hands,
Placing the manifold within the manifold."

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Blessed by All Actions, Arisen from the Pledge of the Great Bodhisattva Duryodhanavīrya, and drew forth from his own heart this essence of all the tathāgatas, called the Pledge of the Protection of All Tathāgatas:

vajra rakṣa

As soon as it had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that very Blessed One Vajrapāṇi. Becoming firm armor, it came forth, entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, and blended into one. Then it became the form of a great vajra armor and rested in his two hands. Then from that form of vajra armor there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed the act of extending the armor of protection over all the tathāgatas and the rest, and the magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is supreme diligence, hard to overcome, and utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the great bodhisattva Duryodhanavīrya. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! The armor of diligence,
Firm by nature, I am supremely firm;
Because it is firm, though it has no body,
It is supreme at making a vajra body."

Then that body of the great bodhisattva Duryodhanavīrya, of great diligence, came forth from the heart of the Blessed One, took its place on a moon maṇḍala to the right of all the tathāgatas, and again sat awaiting his command.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Firmness of All Tathāgatas. For the sake of these things, namely the pledge of the perfection of diligence of all the tathāgatas, the complete protection of the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, the experience of every happiness and mental joy, and so on up to the accomplishment of the supreme attainment of the vajra body of all the tathāgatas, he placed that vajra armor in the same way into the hands of the great bodhisattva Duryodhanavīrya. Then all the tathāgatas consecrated him with the vajra-name consecration, "Vajramitra, Vajramitra." Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajramitra, clad all the tathāgatas in armor with that vajra armor and uttered this inspired utterance:

"This, for all the buddhas,
Is the supreme armor of loving-kindness;
Through firm diligence it is a great protection,
And is declared to be the great friend."

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Blessed by Action, Arisen from the Pledge of the Great Bodhisattva Sarvamārapramardin, and drew forth from his own heart this essence of all the tathāgatas, called the Pledge of the Skillful Means of All Tathāgatas:

vajra yakṣa

Then, as soon as it had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. Becoming a great weapon of vajra fangs, it came forth, entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, and blended into one. Then it became the form of vajra fangs and rested in his two hands.

Then from that form of vajra fangs there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the fine atoms of all the world systems. They performed the magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, such as the taming by the wrathful one of all the tathāgatas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is the crushing of all māras and is utterly firm, they blended into one and became the body of the great bodhisattva Sarvamārapramardin. Dwelling in the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the great means
Of the compassionate buddhas;
Though they are peaceful, for the sake of beings
They come to show themselves as fierce."

Then that body of the great bodhisattva Sarvamārapramardin came forth from the heart of the Blessed One, took its place on a moon maṇḍala to the left of all the tathāgatas, and again sat awaiting his command.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Utterly Wrathful Vajra. For the sake of these results, namely the pledge of all the tathāgatas to tame the wicked, making the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, free from fear, the experience of every happiness and mental joy, and so on up to the attainment and supreme accomplishment of the great means, the wisdom and the superknowledges of all the tathāgatas, he placed that weapon of vajra fangs in the same way into the hands of that great bodhisattva Sarvamārapramardin. Then all the tathāgatas consecrated him with the vajra-name consecration, "Vajracaṇḍa, Vajracaṇḍa." Then the great bodhisattva Vajracaṇḍa placed that weapon of vajra fangs at his own heart and, praising all the tathāgatas, uttered this inspired utterance:

"This, for all the buddhas,
Is the supreme tamer of all the wicked;
It is the sharp weapon of vajra fangs,
The very nature of skillful means and compassion."

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Blessed by Action, Arisen from the Pledge of the Great Bodhisattva of All Tathāgatas called Vajramuṣṭi, and from his own heart there came forth this essence of all the tathāgatas, called the Pledge of the Vajra Binding of the Body, Speech and Mind of All Tathāgatas:

vajra sandhi

Then, as soon as it had come forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, this was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. Becoming the binding of all the mudrās of all the tathāgatas, it came forth, entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, and blended into one. Then it became the form of the vajra binding and rested in his two hands. Then from that form of the vajra binding there came forth bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. They performed throughout all the world systems the mudrās, the wisdom and the rest of all the tathāgatas, and the magical feats and miraculous displays of all the buddhas, and then, because the samādhi of Vajrasattva is the utterly bound fist of all the tathāgatas and is supremely firm, they blended into one and became the body of the great bodhisattva called Sarvatathāgatamuṣṭi. Entering the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, he uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the pledge
Of the utterly firm binding, firm by nature;
In order to fulfill every aspiration completely,
I bind even those who are liberated."

Then that body of the great bodhisattva Sarvatathāgatamuṣṭi came forth from the heart of the Blessed One, took its place on a moon maṇḍala behind all the tathāgatas, and again sat awaiting his command.

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Pledge Vajra of All Tathāgatas. For the sake of these results, namely the pledge of binding the mudrās of all the tathāgatas, the examination of intentions and every accomplishment by all the tathāgatas and the deities for the realms of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, the experience of every happiness and mental joy, and so on up to the supreme accomplishment of the omniscient wisdom of all the tathāgatas and of mastery over the mudrās, he placed that vajra binding in the same way into the hands of the great bodhisattva called Sarvatathāgatamuṣṭi. Then all the tathāgatas consecrated him with the vajra-name consecration, "Vajramuṣṭi, Vajramuṣṭi." Then the great bodhisattva called Vajramuṣṭi, binding all the tathāgatas with that vajra binding, uttered this inspired utterance:

"This, for all the buddhas,
Is the binding of mudrā, great and firm;
So that all the buddhas may swiftly accomplish,
Its pledge is exceedingly hard to transgress."

The action of the extensive rite of offering to all the tathāgatas, the firm armor of great diligence, the great means of all the tathāgatas, and the wisdom that binds all the mudrās: these four are the beings of the great activity of all the tathāgatas.

The second fascicle.

Then the tathāgata called Akṣobhya, having accomplished the wisdom of all the tathāgatas belonging to the Tathāgata, the Blessed One Vairocana, entered, in order to bless the wisdom of all the tathāgatas with mudrā, into the samādhi called Vajra Blessing, arisen from the pledge of the vajra perfection, and drew forth from his own heart this mudrā of all the tathāgatas, called the Vajra Pledge of All Tathāgatas:

satva vajrī

As soon as this had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it became rays of vajra light and came forth, and it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara.

From those rays of light, moreover, there arose the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. Having blessed with mudrā the wisdoms of the vajra perfection of all the tathāgatas, they blended into one again and became a great vajra form, its size equal to all the world systems. Taking its place on a moon maṇḍala in front of the Blessed One Vairocana, it uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the firm self,
The being's vajra of all the buddhas;
For through firmness, though without a body,
It has become a vajra body."

Then the tathāgata called Ratnasaṃbhava, in order to bless with mudrā the wisdom of all the tathāgatas belonging to the Tathāgata, the Blessed One Vairocana, entered into the samādhi called Vajra Blessing, arisen from the pledge of the jewel perfection, and drew forth from his own heart this mudrā of his own, called the Pledge of the Vajra Jewel:

ratna vajrī

Then, as soon as this had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it became rays of jewel light and came forth, and it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. From those rays of jewel light, moreover, there arose the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. Having blessed with mudrā the wisdoms of all the tathāgatas, they blended into one again and became the form of a great vajra jewel, its size equal to all the world systems. Taking its place on a moon maṇḍala to the right of the Blessed One Vairocana, it uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am renowned
As the vajra jewel of all the buddhas;
For of all the mudrās
The way of consecration is supreme."

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Lokeśvararāja, in order to bless with mudrā the wisdom of all the tathāgatas belonging to the Tathāgata, the Blessed One Vairocana, entered into the samādhi of vajra blessing, arisen from the pledge of the Dharma perfection, and drew forth from his own heart this mudrā of his own, called the Pledge of Dharma:

dharmma vajrī

Then, as soon as this had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it became rays of lotus light and came forth, and it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. From those rays of lotus light, moreover, there arose the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. Having blessed with mudrā the wisdoms of all the tathāgatas, they blended into one again and became the form of a great vajra lotus, its size equal to all the world systems. Taking its place on a moon maṇḍala behind the Blessed One Vairocana, it uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the pure
Dharma vajra of all the buddhas;
For, because its nature is completely pure,
Though it is desire, it is without stain."

Then the Blessed One Amoghasiddhi, in order to bless with mudrā the wisdom of all the tathāgatas belonging to the Tathāgata, the Blessed One Vairocana, entered into the samādhi called Vajra Blessing, arisen from the pledge of the action perfection, and drew forth from his own heart this mudrā of his own, called the Pledge of the Action of All Tathāgatas:

karmma vajrī

Then, as soon as it had come forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it became rays of light of all actions, and it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. From those rays of action light, moreover, there arose the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems. Having blessed with mudrā the wisdoms of all the tathāgatas, they blended into one again and became the form of a great action vajra, its size equal to all the world systems, with its points facing in every direction. Taking its place on a moon maṇḍala to the left of the Blessed One Vairocana, it uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the manifold
Action vajra of all the buddhas;
For though it is one, for the realm of beings
Without exception it performs good actions."

The pledge of the wisdom of all the tathāgatas, the great consecration, the vajra nature of reality, and all offerings: these are the great vajra perfections of all the tathāgatas.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Arisen from the Pledge of the Offering that Delights All Tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this great goddess of the family of all the tathāgatas:

vajra lāsye

Then, as soon as this had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it became a vajra mudrā and came forth, and it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. From the points of that vajra mudrā, moreover, there arose the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. The body of the great goddess, the vajra-holding goddess, was in every way like Vajrasattva, with manifold colors, forms, signs and postures, adorned with every ornament, gathering in all the tathāgata families. Having become the goddess of Vajrasattva, she took her place on a moon maṇḍala on the left side of the maṇḍala of the Blessed One Akṣobhya and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! Among the self-born ones
There is no other offering like me,
For through the offering of delight in desire
Every offering comes to be made."

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Arisen from the Pledge of the Consecration with the Jewel Garland of All Tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this great goddess of the family of all the tathāgatas:

vajra māle

Then, as soon as this had come forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it became the mudrā of a great jewel and came forth, and it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. From those mudrās of the great jewel, moreover, there arose the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having likewise become the great goddess Vajramālā, she took her place on a moon maṇḍala on the left side of the maṇḍala of the Blessed One Ratnasaṃbhava and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! None is renowned like me,
Who am called the jewel offering,
For even to the supreme victors of the three realms
I come to make offering in full."

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Arisen from the Pledge of the Supreme Song of All Tathāgatas, and drew forth from his heart this great goddess of the family of all the tathāgatas:

vajra gīti

Then, as soon as this had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it became the Dharma mudrā of all the tathāgatas and came forth, and it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. From those Dharma mudrās of all the tathāgatas, moreover, there arose the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the great goddess Vajragītā, she took her place on a moon maṇḍala on the left side of the maṇḍala of the Blessed One Lokeśvararāja and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the offering
To the all-seeing ones in the manner of supreme song;
For, being like an echo,
I delight them with offerings."

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Arisen from the Pledge of the Dance Offering of All Tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this great goddess of the family of all the tathāgatas:

vajra nṛti

Then, as soon as it had come forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it became the extensive rite of the dance offering of all the tathāgatas and came forth, and it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara. From all those extensive rites of the dance offering of all the tathāgatas, moreover, there arose the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the great goddess Vajranṛtyā, she took her place on a moon maṇḍala on the left side of the maṇḍala of the Tathāgata Amoghasiddhi and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the vast offering
That accomplishes the aim of every offering;
For through the rite of vajra dance
I make offering to the buddhas in full."

The unsurpassed pledge of the happiness and mental joy of all the tathāgatas, the garland of all the tathāgatas, the song of all the tathāgatas, and the unsurpassed offering action of all the tathāgatas: these are the secret offerings of all the tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Akṣobhya, in order to make offering in return for the offering made by the Tathāgata Vairocana, entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Arisen from the Pledge of the Utter Satisfaction of All Tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this chief of the handmaidens of all the tathāgatas:

vajra dhūpe

As soon as this had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, it was that very Blessed One Vajradhara; it came forth pervading everything in vajra form with many clouds of incense offering of many kinds. From those oceans of clouds of incense offering, moreover, there came forth the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the body of the goddess Vajradhūpā, she took her place on a moon maṇḍala in the left corner of the multistoried pavilion of the Blessed One, whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the great offering,
The virtuous one, full of utter satisfaction;
For through beings being endowed with the descent,
Awakening is swiftly attained."

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Ratnasaṃbhava, in order to make offering in return for the offering made by the Tathāgata Vairocana, entered into the samādhi called the Vajra Arisen from the Pledge of the Offering of Jewel Ornaments, and drew forth from his own heart this attendant of all the tathāgatas:

vajra puṣpe

As soon as this had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, that very Blessed One Vajradhara came forth as an array of offerings of every kind of flower, pervading all the world systems. From that array of offerings of every flower there came forth the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the body of the goddess Vajrapuṣpā, she took her place on a moon maṇḍala in the left corner of the multistoried pavilion of the Blessed One, whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I, the offering of flowers,
Am the adornment of all;
For by making offering one swiftly
Attains the jewel-nature of the tathāgatas."

Then the Blessed One, the tathāgata Lokeśvararāja, in order to make offering in return for the offering of the Blessed One Vairocana, entered into the samādhi called the vajra arising from the samaya of the offering of illumination to all the tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this female messenger of all the tathāgatas:

vajra āloke

Then, as soon as she had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, that very Blessed One Vajradhara came forth as an array of offerings of illumination everywhere, pervading the entire sphere of reality (dharmadhātu). From that array of offerings of illumination everywhere there came forth the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the body of the goddess called Vajrālokā, she took her place on a moon maṇḍala on the left side of the Blessed One, in the corner of the multistoried pavilion whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the lamp of offering,
The vast virtue;
For one who possesses its light swiftly
Obtains the eye of all the buddhas."

Then the Blessed One, the tathāgata Amoghasiddhi, in order to make offering in return for the offering made by the Blessed One, the tathāgata Vairocana, entered into the samādhi called the vajra arising from the samaya of the scent offering of all the tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this female servant of all the tathāgatas:

vajra gandhe

Then, as soon as she had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, that very Blessed One Vajradhara came forth as an array of offerings of every kind of scent, pervading all the world systems. From that array of offerings of every scent there came forth the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the body of the goddess called Vajragandhā, she took her place on a moon maṇḍala in the corner on the left side of the multistoried pavilion of the Blessed One, whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the delightful scent
Of the offering of divine substances;
The scent of the tathāgatas
Will be bestowed upon all bodies."

The scents that bring down the wisdom of all the tathāgatas, that gather the limbs of great awakening, that illuminate everywhere the Dharma of all the tathāgatas, and that reveal moral discipline, samādhi, wisdom, liberation, and the wisdom and vision of liberation, carry out the command of all the tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana again entered into the samādhi called the sattvavajra arising from the samaya of the hook of the samaya of all the tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this lord of all the many mudrās of all the tathāgatas:

vajra aṃkuśa

Then, as soon as he had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, that very Blessed One Vajradhara came forth as all the many mudrās of all the tathāgatas. From those many mudrās of all the tathāgatas there came forth the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the body of the great bodhisattva called Vajrāṅkuśa, he took his place on a moon maṇḍala within the vajra gatehouse of the multistoried pavilion of the Blessed One, whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, and, summoning all the tathāgatas by their samaya, uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the steadfast
True summoner of all the buddhas;
For when I have summoned them,
They will attend every maṇḍala."

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the vajra arising from the samaya of the great being, which leads into the samaya of all the tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this attendant who draws in the mudrās of all the tathāgatas:

vajra pāśa

As soon as he had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, that very Blessed One Vajradhara came forth as many mudrās that draw in the samaya of all the tathāgatas. Then from those many mudrās that draw in the samaya of all the tathāgatas there came forth the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the body of the great bodhisattva called Vajrapāśa, he took his place on a moon maṇḍala within the gatehouse of the multistoried pavilion of the Blessed One, whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, and, drawing in all the tathāgatas, uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the firm
Vajra noose of all the buddhas;
Even all who dwell in the minute atoms
I shall draw in once again."

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the sattvavajra arising from the samaya of the great being, the chain of the samaya of all the tathāgatas, and drew forth from his own heart this messenger of all the tathāgatas, who binds the samaya of all the tathāgatas:

vajra sphoṭa

Then, as soon as he had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, that very Blessed One Vajradhara came forth as many mudrās that bind the samaya of all the tathāgatas.

From those many mudrās that bind the samaya of all the tathāgatas there came forth the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one again. Having become the body of the great bodhisattva called Vajrasphoṭa, he took his place on a moon maṇḍala within the vajra Dharma gatehouse of the multistoried pavilion of the Blessed One, whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, and, binding all the tathāgatas, uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the firm
Vajra chain of all the buddhas;
Those who are freed from every bond
I wish to bind for the sake of beings."

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the sattvavajra arising from the samaya of the great being, called causing all the tathāgatas to enter, and drew forth from his own heart this servant of all the mudrās of all the tathāgatas:

vajra āveśa

Then, as soon as he had been drawn forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, that very Blessed One Vajradhara came forth as all the many mudrās of all the tathāgatas. From all those many mudrās of all the tathāgatas there came forth the bodies of tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, and they blended into one. Having become the body of the great bodhisattva Vajrāveśa, he took his place on a moon maṇḍala within the vajra karma gatehouse of the multistoried pavilion of the Blessed One, whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, and, causing all the tathāgatas to enter, uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the steadfast
Vajra entering of all the buddhas;
Though I have become the master of all,
I have also become a servant."

The summoning, the drawing in, the binding and the bringing under control of all the tathāgatas carry out the command of all the tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One, in order to bless the gathering of all the tathāgatas, gave the signal with a vajra snap of the fingers and spoke this blessing that gathers all the tathāgatas:

vajra samājaḥ

Then in an instant, a moment, a second, at that very time, roused by the signal of the finger snap of all the tathāgatas, from all the clouds of the boundless oceans of world systems, tathāgatas as numerous as the finest atoms of all the world systems, together with their many maṇḍalas of bodhisattva retinues, set out and went to where the multistoried pavilion with its pinnacle made of gems and jewels stood, to where the Blessed One, the tathāgata Vairocana, was, and uttered this inspired utterance:

oṃ sarvva tathāgata pāda vandanaṃ karomi

So they said. Reciting as they wished that mantra, accomplished by its own nature, and bowing at the feet of all the tathāgatas, they uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful the bodhisattvas'
Supreme conduct of Samantabhadra!
Beautiful is the circle of the tathāgatas;
Beautiful the tathāgata at its center."

When they had said this, all those tathāgatas who had gathered from all the world systems of the ten directions, together with all their maṇḍalas of bodhisattva retinues, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, entered into the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana. Then they came forth from the heart of all the tathāgatas, each together with his own maṇḍala of bodhisattva retinue, and, having formed maṇḍalas in all directions of the Blessed One's multistoried pavilion whose pinnacle is made of vajra, gems and jewels, they remained there in meditative absorption and uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! The vastness of all the buddhas
Has from the very beginning been unborn;
The buddhas, as many as all the atoms,
Become one."

Then all the blessed tathāgatas assembled once more, and, in order to protect fully the realm of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, to bestow benefit and happiness upon all, and so on up to accomplishing the wisdom of equality of all the tathāgatas, the superknowledges, and supreme, perfect and complete awakening, they petitioned the Blessed One, the lord of all the tathāgatas, their own Vajrasattva, without beginning or end, the great Vajradhara, to bless this great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu, with these hundred and eight names:

"Vajrasattva, great being,
Vajra, all the tathāgatas,
Vajra first one, Samantabhadra:
Homage to you, Vajrapāṇi!"

"Vajrarāja, supreme buddha,
Vajrāṅkuśa, tathāgata,
Amogharāja, supreme vajra:
Homage to you, Vajra Summoner!

Vajrarāga, great bliss,
Vajra arrow that brings under control,
Desire of Māra, great vajra:
Homage to you, Vajra Bow!

Vajrasādhu, supreme being,
