Vajra satisfaction, great joy,
King of delight, supreme vajra:
Homage to you, Vajra Delight!

Vajra jewel, vajra aim,
Vajra sky, great gem,
Ākāśagarbha, vajra wealth:
Homage to you, Vajragarbha!

Vajra splendor, blazing greatly,
Vajra sun, light of the victors,
Vajra rays, great splendor:
Homage to you, Vajra Light!

Vajra supreme glory, aim of beings,
Vajraketu, giver of great delight,
Great vajra, Jewel Glory:
Homage to you, Vajra Glory!

Vajra laughter, great laughter,
Vajra smile, great wonder,
Joyful and delighted, shower of the vajra:
Homage to Vajrahāsa!

Vajra Dharma, supreme, meaning of suchness,
Vajra lotus, giver of great delight,
Supreme Lokeśvara, Vajranetra:
Homage to you, Vajranetra!

Vajra sharpness, Great Vehicle,
Vajra sword, great weapon,
Mañjuśrī, vajra most profound:
Homage to you, Vajrabuddhi!

Vajra cause and great essence,
Vajra wheel, great way,
Turning fully, arisen from the vajra:
Homage to Vajragarbha!

Vajra speech, supreme self-awareness,
Vajra recitation, giver of full accomplishment,
Speechless one, supreme vajra accomplishment:
Homage to you, Vajra Speech!

Vajra supreme action, vajra command,
Action vajra, going everywhere,
Vajra certainty, vast:
Homage to you, Vajra All!

Vajra protection, great steadfastness,
Vajra armor, great firmness,
Supreme diligence, hard to overcome:
Homage to Vajra Diligence!

Vajra yakṣa, great skillful means,
Vajra fang, great terror,
Vajra destroyer of Māra, unbearable:
Homage to Vajra Fierce One!

Vajra joining, deeply bowing,
Vajra bond, fully releasing,
Vajramuṣṭi, supreme samaya:
Homage to Vajramuṣṭi!

Whoever holds these names,
These hundred and eight peaceful ones,
Is empowered by all the supreme ones
With the vajra name empowerment and the rest.

Whoever, with these names of qualities,
Constantly sings in praise
Of the great Vajradhara,
Will himself become like Vajradhara.

With these hundred and eight names
We have praised you.
Please expand once more the great way
Of realizing the Great Vehicle.

Protector, we beseech you:
Please explain the supreme rite
Of the great and sacred maṇḍala,
The great circle of all the buddhas."

Then the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, having heard the words of petition of all the tathāgatas, entered into the samādhi called blessed by the vajra blessing arising from the samaya of all the tathāgatas, and explained this great maṇḍala called Vajradhātu:

"Next I shall properly explain
The sacred vajra maṇḍala.
Because it resembles the vajra sphere,
It is known by the name Vajradhātu.

Meditating vastly on the mudrā
Of the great being, and performing the blessing,
Having entered the very center
Of the maṇḍala in the proper way,

Abiding in the mudrā, one rises up just so,
And having looked all around,
Saying 'Vajrasattva,'
One circles it in a stately manner.

Having stretched a new cord,
The wise one, as well as he can,
With a line well proportioned and beautiful,
Should mark out the lines of the maṇḍala.

Make it square, with four gates,
Beautify it with four portals,
Endowed with four lines,
And adorn it with silk and garlands of flowers.

In all the corners
And within the gate recesses,
Adorned with vajras and jewels,
Mark out the outer enclosure of the maṇḍala.

Moving within that enclosure,
Encircle it, like a wheel,
With a line of vajras.
Beautify it with eight pillars.

On the inner side of the vajra pillars
Adorn it with five moon maṇḍalas.
In the center of the central maṇḍala
Set the image of the Buddha.

In the centers of the maṇḍalas
On all sides of the Buddha,
In due order, the four supreme samayas
Should be properly set.

Into those four maṇḍalas,
Going with vajra steps,
Set all four buddhas,
Akṣobhya and the rest.

In the maṇḍala of Akṣobhya
Set Vajradhara and the others with him;
In the maṇḍala of Ratnasaṃbhava
Set Vajragarbha and the others, complete;

In the maṇḍala of Amitāyus
Set Vajranetra and the others, pure;
In the maṇḍala of Amoghasiddhi
Set Viśvavajra and the others.

In the corners of the wheel
Set the vajra goddesses as well;
In the corners of the outer enclosure
Set the offering goddesses of the buddhas as well.

Within the gates
The four gatekeepers too should be set;
On the sides of the outer enclosure
Set the great beings as well.

Then the vajra master,
Having bound and entered, according to the rite,
The mudrā of the supreme samaya,
Should cause the descent with the bell mudrā."

Here the heart mantra that causes everyone to descend is this:

aḥ

With "Bless me" and the rest, one receives the command just as it is given.

"Having done so, one states one's own name.
Then, accomplishing with the vajra,
The vajra master, having bound
The vajra hook of the sattva,

Should strike many snaps of the fingers
And gather all the buddhas.
Then all the buddhas,
Together with Vajrasattva and the rest,

Filling all the maṇḍalas,
Will assemble in the maṇḍalas.
Then, meditating on
The great mudrā of Vajrasattva,

One should recite a little
Of the sacred hundred and eight names.
Then, because the assembly is pleased,
The tathāgatas become steadfast.

Vajrasattva, accomplished of himself,
Abides close by as a friend.
Then, at all the gates,
Having performed the actions of the hook and the rest,

With the mudrā of the supreme great action
One should set the samayas.
With the mudrā of the supreme samaya
And with 'sattvavajra' and the rest,

Accomplish the great beings in this way,
Saying jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ.
Then, to gather all the great beings,
The buddhas and the rest,

Invite them and cause them to enter fully;
When they are bound, they come under one's control.
Then, having pleased the great beings
With the secret offering,

For the accomplishment of everything, one should request,
'Please accomplish all the aims of beings.'"

In all the maṇḍalas the vajra master performs the rite in the same way.

Then, in this great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu, there is an extensive rite for the vajra disciple's entering the maṇḍala and so on. Here, first, there is the entering. It is for the sake of fully protecting the realm of sentient beings, entirely and without remainder, and of accomplishing the supreme benefit and happiness of all beings. For entering this great maṇḍala one need not examine whether a person is a fit vessel or not. Why is that? Blessed One, there are some beings who commit great evil; if they enter that great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu and see it, they will be freed from all the lower realms.

Blessed One, there are also beings who are attached to wealth and gems, to food and drink, and to all the objects of sensual pleasure, who take no delight in the samaya, and who are incapable of the preliminary practices and the like. They too, entering there and acting as they wish, will have all the aspirations of those who enter completely fulfilled.

Blessed One, there are some beings who delight in dance, song, laughter and play, in food and enjoyments, and who, because they have not understood the nature of reality, the realization of the Great Vehicle of all the tathāgatas, enter the families and maṇḍalas of other gods. And there are also those who, frightened and terrified by the words of the precepts, do not enter that maṇḍala of the family of all the tathāgatas, where, because all hopes are truly and completely fulfilled, unsurpassed joy, satisfaction and delight arise. So that they too, who stand facing toward entering the path of the maṇḍala of the lower realms, may accomplish everything sublime in joy and satisfaction, may experience happiness and mental ease, and may be turned back from the path that faces toward entering all the courses of the lower realms, it is fitting that they enter that great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu.

Blessed One, there are also beings with an affinity for the Dharma who, seeking the awakening of the buddhas through the means of accomplishing the sublime moral discipline, samādhi and wisdom of all the tathāgatas, strive through the dhyānas, the liberations and the grounds, and grow weary. For them too, as soon as they enter that great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu, even the accomplishment of becoming all the tathāgatas is not difficult; what need, then, to speak of other accomplishments?

Here, first of all, one should make four prostrations to all the tathāgatas. It is done as follows. Stretching out the vajra joined palms, one prostrates with the whole body and says this mantra:

oṃ sarvva tathāgata pūjopsthānāya ātnānaṃ niryātayāmi sarvva tathāgata vajra satva adhitiṣṭha svamāṃ

Then, remaining in the same way, placing the vajra joined palms at the heart, one prostrates with the forehead and says this mantra:

oṃ sarvva tathāgata būjā abhiṣekāya ātpānaṃ niryātayāmi sarvva tathāgata vajra ratna abhiṣiñca maṃ

Then, remaining in the same way, joining the vajra palms at the crown, one prostrates with the mouth and says this:

oṃ sarvva tathāgata pūjā pravartanāya ātmānaṃ niryātayāmi sarvva tathāgata vajra dharmma pravartaya maṃ

Then, remaining in the same way, lowering the vajra joined palms from the crown and placing them at the heart, one prostrates with the crown of the head and says this:

oṃ sarvva tathāgata pūjā karmmaṇe ātmānaṃ niryātayāmi sarvva tathāgata vajra karmma kuru māṃ

Then the disciple puts on a red upper garment, the face is bound with a red cloth, and, binding the mudrā of the sattvavajra, he recites this heart mantra:

sam yastvaṃ

Then, having him hold a garland of flowers between his two middle fingers, one leads him in, reciting this heart mantra:

samaya hūṃ

Then, as soon as he has entered, instruct him thus: "Today you enter the family of all the tathāgatas. I shall generate in you such wisdom that by it you will attain even the accomplishment of becoming all the tathāgatas; what need, then, to speak of other accomplishments? Do not speak of this to those who have not seen the great maṇḍala, lest your samaya be broken."

Then the vajra master himself should bind the mudrā of the sattvavajra, shown turned inward and turned outward, place it on the crown of the vajra disciple's head, and say this: "This is your vajra samaya. If you speak of it to anyone, your head will split."

Then, having consecrated the water of the oath with that same samaya mudrā and with the heart mantra of the oath, he has the disciple drink it. The heart mantra of the oath is this:

"Today Vajrasattva
Enters truly into your heart.
If you speak of this way,
At once he will burst forth and depart.

oṃ vajrodkatha"

Then instruct the disciple thus: "From this day on I am your Vajrapāṇi. Whatever I command you, saying, 'Do this,' you must do."

"Do not despise me, lest, by not avoiding what is perilous, you die and fall into hell." Having said this, one has him say: "May all the tathāgatas bless me, and may Vajrasattva descend upon me."

Then the vajra master quickly binds the mudrā of the sattvavajra and has him say this:

"This is the vajra samaya,
Renowned as Vajrasattva.
May the unsurpassed vajra wisdom
Descend this very day!

oṃ vajra āveśa aḥ"

Then, in wrathful mode, one binds the mudrā, knots and draws the mudrā of the sattvavajra, and has him utter, as far as he is able, words of the realization of the Great Vehicle. Then one causes the descent, and as soon as it has descended, divine wisdom arises in him. Through that wisdom he comes to know the minds of others. He comes to know all the intentions of the past, the future and the present. Through it his mind becomes firm in all the teachings of the tathāgatas. Through it all his suffering comes to an end. Free of every fear, he becomes beyond harm. He becomes blessed by all the sattvas and by all the tathāgatas. All accomplishments become directly manifest to him. Without cause, every bliss arises in him that brings joy, satisfaction and delight never known before. Through some of these blisses samādhi is accomplished; through some, dhāraṇī; through some, all hopes are completely fulfilled; and so on, up to: through some, the state of all the tathāgatas is accomplished.

Then, binding that mudrā and releasing it at his own heart, one has him say this heart mantra as well:

tiṣṭha vajra dṛḍho me bhava śāśvatyo me bhava dridayame adhitiṣṭha sarba siddhimme brayacca hūṃ ha ha ha ha ho

Then one has him cast the garland of flowers onto the great maṇḍala and utter this heart mantra as well:

pratīccha vajra hoḥ

Then whatever it falls upon, through that he will attain accomplishment. Then, taking up the garland, one ties it on his head and has him say this heart mantra:

oṃ pratigrihṇa svamimāṃ satva mahvabala

Because it has been tied on, he has been made his own by that great being, and so he will swiftly attain accomplishment.

Then, while the descent is still upon him, one unties the covering of his face and has him say this heart mantra as well:

oṃ vajra satva sayaṃ tetya cakṣurgatana tatpara utghaṭa yati sarvvākṣo vajra cakṣu anuttaraṃ he vajra pāśya

Then one shows him the great maṇḍala in due order. As soon as he sees the great maṇḍala he becomes blessed by all the tathāgatas, and Vajrasattva too abides in his heart. Then, because of the blessing of all the tathāgatas, he sees maṇḍalas of light rays of many kinds and the like, and he also sees miraculous powers and wondrous displays. It may be that the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara himself, or the body of a tathāgata, will appear to him. From then on, every aim, every wish of the mind, every accomplishment, up to the very state of Vajradhara or of a tathāgata, will be accomplished.

Then, after he has seen the great maṇḍala, one should empower him with the scented water in a vase blessed with the vajra, and with this heart mantra:

oṃ vajra abhiṣiñca mī

Then, binding whichever mudrā or garland is suitable, and placing his own emblem in his hand, one should say this:

"The vajra empowerment of all the buddhas
I shall confer on you today.
For full accomplishment as all the buddhas,
Take up this vajra.

oṃ vajra adhipati stvaṃ abhiṣiñcāmi tiththa vajra samaya stvaṃ"

Then, with this heart mantra, one should confer the empowerment called the vajra name:

oṃ vajra satva stvaṃ abhiṣiñcāmi vajra nāma abhiṣeka ta

Whatever name he is given, as "O Vajra so-and-so," one should address him with "O."

This is the extensive rite for entering all the maṇḍalas.

Then one says to him: "What do you wish: the wisdom of the accomplishment that brings forth wealth, the wisdom that achieves the accomplishment of miraculous power, the wisdom that achieves the accomplishment of a vidyādhara, and so on up to the wisdom that achieves the supreme accomplishment of all the tathāgatas?" Then one should explain to him whatever each one wishes.

Then one should teach the mudrā wisdom that achieves the accomplishment of all aims:

"One should meditate fully, at the heart,
On the Vajradhātu, where treasure abides.
Having meditated, he will see
The treasures that lie in the earth.

Having drawn the shape of a vajra,
One should meditate on it on the face of the sky.
Wherever it is seen to fall,
There, one should know, is treasure.

When a wise person meditates
On the shape of a vajra upon his tongue,
He himself will say, truthfully,
'It is here.'

Having meditated on one's own body,
Wholly, in the shape of a vajra,
Wherever one stands once the descent has come,
There, it is taught, treasure lies."

Their heart mantras are these:

oṃ vajra nidhī oṃ ratna nidhī oṃ dharmma niddī oṃ karmma nidhī

Then one should teach the mudrā wisdom that achieves the accomplishment of vajra miraculous power:

"Having performed the vajra descent,
And meditated on the water as having the shape of a vajra,
One swiftly gains the accomplishment
Of going across upon the water.

Having performed the descent in this way,
If one meditates on one's own body
In the form of anyone at all,
One becomes even the body of a buddha.

Having caused the descent upon oneself in this way,
If one meditates on oneself as space,
For as long as one wishes,
For so long one becomes invisible.

Having become one into whom the vajra has descended,
If one meditates on oneself as being a vajra,
For as long as one wishes to soar from where one stands,
For so long one travels through the sky."

Their heart mantras are these:

vajra jvala vajra rūpa vajra ākāśa vajra mahūṃ

Then one should train in the gnosis of the mudrās that accomplish the siddhi of a vajra vidyādhara:

Having drawn the shape of a moon,
One should leap up into the sky above.
If one meditates on a vajra in one's hand,
One becomes a vajra vidyādhara.

Remaining seated upon the shape of a moon,
If one meditates intently on the vajra jewel,
For as long as one wishes oneself to rise,
For so long, in an instant, one soars aloft.

Seated upon the shape of a moon,
If one meditates on abiding on the vajra lotus,
The vajra eye
Bestows the rank of a vidyādhara.

Abiding in the center of the moon maṇḍala,
If one meditates on the action vajra,
Then, from holding the crossed vajra,
One swiftly becomes a universal vidyādhara.

Their heart mantras are these: vajradhara ratnadhara dharmmadhara karmmadhara

Then one should train in the gnosis of the mudrās that accomplish the supreme siddhi of all the tathāgatas:

If one imagines the samādhi of all vajras
In the expanse of space,
Then, as a vajra self, for as long as one wishes,
For so long one soars aloft.

If one meditates on the samādhi
Pure of everything, likewise unsurpassed,
Through accomplishing gnosis one swiftly
Attains the five superknowledges.

If one recollects all of space
As resembling Vajrasattva,
Oneself endowed with stable mindfulness,
One swiftly becomes Vajradhara.

Focusing on the whole expanse of space
As the forms of buddhas,
Through the samādhi of all buddhas
One is accomplished as a buddha.

Their heart mantras are these: vajra vajra śuddha śuddha satva satva buddha buddha. These are the accomplishments of the gnosis of all the siddhis.

Then, since this is a matter of keeping secret, one should first pronounce to him the heart of the oath: oṃ vajrasatva svayandye hṛdyes mabsta tani vīryā tata kṣaṇāṃ yāyā dyati bruyad idam nayaṃ. Then one should command him thus: "Do not transgress this heart of the oath. Should you do so, not having avoided harm, you will die an untimely death, and in this very body you will fall into hell."

Then one should train in the gnosis of the secret mudrās:

Having performed the vajra descent,
If, composed, one gently strikes
With the face of the vajra palms,
Even a mountain comes under one's control.

This is the mudrā of the vajra palms.

By the rite of the vajra descent,
Through the practice of striking gently
With the face of the vajra bond,
One summons even a mountain entirely.

Likewise, by the rite of the descent,
Having extended the vajra bond,
If one strikes the fingertips together,
A hundred families are destroyed in a moment.

By the rite of the subtle descent,
Placing all the fingers evenly
And releasing them from the vajra bond
Is supreme in dispelling all suffering.

Then comes the accomplishment of their secret:

One should cause it to enter
The opening of a woman or a man.
Having entered thus by means of the mind,
Imagine it pervading their entire body.

The heart mantras of the palms for this are these: vajrapāśa vajra āveśa vajra hana vajra hara

Then one should train in the gnosis of the four mudrās of the deity of one's own family. As to the rite, one should command thus: "Do not show any mudrā whatsoever to anyone else who does not know the mudrās. Why is that? Because if beings who have not seen the great maṇḍala bind the mudrās, they will not then be accomplished in that way. Then they will fall into doubt, and, not having avoided harm, they will die very swiftly and fall into the great hell called Avīci. And you yourself will go to the lower realms."

Then there is the gnosis of the great mudrā that accomplishes the being of all the tathāgatas:

Beginning from the realization of the mind,
Reciting "Vajradhātu,"
Transforming oneself into the body of a buddha,
One should meditate on the vajra sun.

As soon as one is accomplished, by that
One gains gnosis, life, strength, youth,
And the power to go everywhere;
Even the accomplishment of buddhahood is not hard.

This is the mudrā of the complete and manifest awakening of all the tathāgatas.

Then there is the binding of the great mudrā that accomplishes Vajrasattva:

With body, speech, and mind as vajra,
Generating the mind of vajra pride,
If, with that pride, one brandishes the vajra,
One becomes Vajrasattva oneself.

Thereby the power to go everywhere,
Lordship over all desires, blissfulness,
Great miraculous power, strength, and supreme form
Become like those of Vajrasattva.

With body, speech, and mind as vajra,
In conformity with the painted image,
Endowed with the mudrās of the emblems,
One should accomplish oneself as the great being.

Here the practices of all the sections,
The siddhis, and the great acts
Of those who are accomplished
I shall explain in their proper order.

First, as at the daily sessions,
Having performed self-blessing and the rest,
One should accomplish everything;
After that, one may do as one pleases.

Here is the extensive rite for accomplishing the great mudrā:

Having performed the vajra descent,
And bound the great mudrā according to the rite,
One meditates on that great being
In the space before oneself.

Having seen that gnosis being,
One should meditate on it in one's own body.
Summoning it, causing it to enter, binding it,
And bringing it under control, one should accomplish it.

Their heart mantras are these: vajrasatva aḥrdo, the heart mantra of the vajra descent. vajrasatva dṛśya ho, the heart mantra for recollecting the great being. jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ, the heart mantra for inviting, causing to enter, binding, and bringing under control the great being.

By saying samaya stvaṃ,
Having accomplished a moon behind oneself,
There one meditates on one's body as the being,
And says samaya stvaṃ ahaṃ.

Whatever mudrā belongs to whichever being,
Having meditated on that as oneself,
One should accomplish it through vajra recitation.
This is the accomplishment of all the mudrās.

Furthermore, as the great accomplishment,
One says jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ,
And by the excellent rite, by means of the mind,
Causes all the buddhas to enter one's body.

Then come the unsurpassed vajra actions;
I shall explain their actions.
If the recollection of the buddhas is accomplished,
One swiftly becomes a buddha.
If the sattva vajra is accomplished,
One becomes the lord of all the mudrās.
Through the mudrā of the jewel vajra,
One gains power over all jewels.
If the Dharma vajra is accomplished,
One becomes a holder of the Dharma of the buddhas.
Through the mudrā of the action vajra,
One performs the vajra actions.

Through binding the mudrā of the being,
One accomplishes Vajrasattva.
Through the rite of the vajra that draws in,
Vajradhara is invited into one's presence.
The great mudrā of the vajra of desire
Delights all the buddhas.
Through the rite of Vajrasādhu,
One gladdens all the buddhas.
Through the rite of the jewel mudrā,
One is likewise empowered by the buddhas.
Through the rite of vajra splendor,
One swiftly accomplishes vajra splendor.
By relying on the holder of vajra glory,
One fulfills all hopes completely.
Through the rite of vajra laughter,
One laughs together with all the buddhas.
Through the rite of the vajra Dharma,
One becomes a holder of the vajra Dharma.
Through the rite of the sharp vajra,
One gains the supreme wisdom of all the buddhas.
By relying on the holder of the vajra wheel,
One turns the wheel of Dharma.
Through the rite of vajra speech,
One attains the accomplishment of the speech of the buddhas.
By accomplishing the supreme vajra action,
One swiftly succeeds in vajra action.
Having donned the vajra armor,
One attains a vajra body.
By accomplishing the vajra yakṣa,
One becomes equal to the vajra yakṣa.
By binding the vajra fist,
One accomplishes all the mudrās.
By accomplishing the vajra holder,
One attains great vajra play.
By binding the vajra garland,
One is empowered by all the buddhas.
Through the rite of vajra song,
One sings vajra songs.
Through the practice of vajra dance,
One thereby makes offerings to all the buddhas.
Through the rite of vajra incense,
One satisfies all beings.
By applying the vajra flower,
One thereby brings beings under control.
The great mudrā of the vajra lamp
Bestows the most supreme eye.
Through the rite of vajra perfume,
One dispels all suffering.
By drawing in with the vajra hook,
One becomes supreme among all who draw in.
Through the rite of the vajra noose,
One causes everything to enter.
Through the practice of the vajra chain,
One becomes able to bind everything.
Through the rite of the vajra descent,
One fully succeeds in making everything descend.

Then the gnosis of the vajra samaya mudrās of all the tathāgatas is explained:

From palms firmly joined,
Interlacing all the fingers
Is declared to be the vajra palms;
Because it is firmly bound, it is the vajra bond.
Since all the samaya mudrās
Arise from the vajra bond,
I shall explain their binding,
The unsurpassed binding of the vajra.

Having made the vajra bond firm,
Raise the middle fingers together like a sprout.
The middle fingers laid together inside
Is declared to be that of the second buddha.
The middle fingers are made like a jewel,
And the middle fingers bent like a lotus.
The mudrā of the fifth buddha
Is likewise the bent index fingers.

Next, of the families of the tathāgatas,
I shall rightly explain the binding
Of the mudrās that hold the samaya,
And their siddhis and actions.

Of the two hands, like the moon,
With the middle fingers released,
Raise the thumbs and the little fingers separately:
This is the sattva vajra; raised, it is the vajra.
The index fingers as a hook, the index fingers joined,
And as though giving "Excellent!":
These are the siddhis of the many mudrās
Of the four vajra beings.

Lay the thumbs together and bend the two index fingers,
Their tips touching: this is the jewel vajra.
That same, with the middle and ring fingers
And little fingers crossed and extended;
From that, the ring and little fingers
Laid evenly together is the banner.
Then, turning this around,
It is placed toward the mouth.

Both thumbs laid evenly together,
Both index fingers bent,
And the middle fingers raised with their tips bent:
That itself is the vajra sword.
From that, the ring and little fingers laid together
Is declared to be the wheel.
Bound, the two thumbs released,
Extended and placed toward the mouth.

Thumbs and little fingers raised with tips touching
Is declared to be the action vajra.
From that, the two index fingers laid together
Are extended and placed at the heart.
The tips of the two index fingers bent,
With the little fingers laid together, is the fang.
The two thumbs upon the two little fingers,
Pressed by the two index fingers.

The thumbs placed together at the heart
And fully extended is the garland.
The joined palms, their tips released from the mouth;
From dance, the joined palms placed at the crown.
The vajra bond poured downward;
The joined palms scattered upward;
The thumbs together, firmly bound;
Fully extended, it is the anointing.

One index finger bent in summoning;
The two thumbs, the knot cast off;
Thumbs and index fingers bound as a fetter;
The vajra bond with the index fingers closed.

Then their accomplishment is explained,
The sublime accomplishment of the vajra:
Through the samādhi of Vajrasattva,
One places one's own mudrā at the heart.

Then their actions are explained,
The unsurpassed vajra actions.
The mudrā of the five of the Vajradhātu,
Through the assembly of the tathāgatas,
Immediately blesses
The maṇḍala master together with the disciple.

By binding the sattva vajra,
One becomes like Vajradhara.
By binding the vajra hook,
One summons even all the buddhas.
By applying the vajra of desire,
One delights the perfect buddhas.
Through vajra joy, one praises
All the Victors with "Excellent!"
By binding the jewel vajra,
One is empowered by all the buddhas.
By binding the vajra sun,
One becomes like the radiance of the buddhas.
Through the vajra banner being raised,
One fulfills all hopes.
In the manner of vajra laughter,
One laughs together with all the buddhas.
By binding the Dharma vajra,
One becomes like the vajra Dharma.
By wielding the vajra sword,
One thereby cuts through all afflictions.
By holding the vajra wheel firm,
One becomes the lord of the maṇḍala.
Through the practice of vajra speech,
One accomplishes supreme vajra speech.
By binding the action vajra,
One becomes like the vajra action.
By making the vajra armor firm,
One's body becomes like a vajra.
The supreme mudrā of the vajra fang
Thereby crushes the savage māras.

By firmly binding the vajra fist,
All the mudrās too come under control.
By the smile, the joys of the gods;
By the garland, the ornaments;
By song, a voice ever clear;
By dance, one obtains offerings.
Incense gladdens the world;
By flowers, a beautiful form;
By the lamp, pure light;
By perfume, divine fragrant substances.
Vajrāṅkuśa summons;
Vajrapāśa causes to enter;
Vajrasphoṭa binds;
And the vajra bell causes descent.

Then the Dharma mudrās are explained.

vajra jñāna: the buddhas
Make the Vajradhātu firm.

Then, further, there is explained
The rite of the Dharma mudrās:
If one says samaya stvaṃ,
One becomes the lord of all the mudrās.
If one says ānaya sva,
One summons the buddhas without fail.
If one says aho sukha,
The buddhas too are delighted.
If one says sādhu sādhu,
One gladdens them with words of praise.
If one says sumahattvaṃ,
One obtains the empowerment of all the buddhas.
If one says rūpa dyot,
One becomes the very splendor of the Dharma.
If one says artha prāpta,
One fulfills all hopes.
If one laughs ha ha hūṃ he,
One obtains the laughter of the buddhas.
If one says sarvva kari,
One accomplishes even what cannot be done.
If one says duḥkh ccheda,
That cuts through all suffering.
If one says buddha bodhi,
One becomes the lord of the maṇḍalas.
If one says prati śabda,
One speaks together with the buddhas.
If one says subhāṣitvaṃ,
One comes to have power over all.
If one says nirbhaya stvaṃ,
One becomes fearless immediately.
If one says śatruṃ bhakṣa,
One devours all enemies.
If one says sarvā siddhi,
One becomes accomplished in everything.

mahā rati: the joy of the gods;
rlupa śobhe: a beautiful form;
śotra saukhya: it bestows happiness;
sarvva pūja: it makes offerings;
prahlādani: one becomes glad of mind;
phalāgāmi: one obtains the fruit;
su tejāgri: great splendor;
sughandhāṃgi: supremely fragrant;
āya ha jiḥ: it summons;
āyāhi hūṃ: it causes to enter;
he sphoṭa baṃ: the great binding;
ghanḍā a aḥrab: it rings out.

Then the virtuous accomplishment of their Dharma mudrās is explained:

Having meditated on a vajra upon the tongue,
One will perform all actions.

Then the binding of the action mudrās is explained:

Having firmly bound the vajra fist,
Through composure, make it twofold.
Having bound the two vajra mudrās,
The binding is then fully explained.
The index finger of the left vajra
Should be raised within the right.
This is the mudrā of supreme awakening,
Which bestows the supreme awakening of the buddhas.
Akṣobhya's touching of the earth;
The jewel one's bestowing of the supreme gift;
The supreme samādhi of Amitāyus;
The granting of fearlessness of Amoghasiddhi.

Then the action mudrās
I shall explain in brief.
The beings, Vajrasattva and the rest,
Perform the vajra actions:
Brandishing proudly with both hands;
Remaining as though holding a hook;
The manner of shooting an arrow;
"Excellent!" placed at the heart.
For empowerment, the two vajras;
The sun made firm at the heart;
On the left, the shape of an elbow;
Likewise turned toward the mouth;
With the right, opening the left;
At the heart, the left; the sword and spear;
Whirling the wheel like a firebrand;
Both vajras released from the mouth;
The vajra dance whirled and released;
The palms at the cheeks, placed at the crown;
The armor; the little fingers as fangs;
And the two fists pressing down.
In the manner of the vajra proud posture,
One torments with stirred-up thought;
Binding the garland, releasing it from the mouth;
And the vajra dance is the whirling.
In the manner of the vajra fist,
Likewise give incense and the rest,
Performing the mudrā offerings
That are offerings to all the buddhas.
Binding the hook with the index finger;
From the little fingers, the great hook;
The wrist knot; the two index fingers a ring;
And the two backs pressed evenly together.

Then their accomplishment is explained,
Equal to performing vajra actions:
The vajra that is the reality of all vajras
Should be meditated upon in one's own heart.

Then the action mudrās,
The vajra actions of many kinds:
By binding the wisdom fist,
The gnosis of the buddhas enters.
By the binding of Akṣobhya,
The mind becomes unshaken.
Through the mudrā of Ratnasaṃbhava,
One brings benefit to others.
Through the mudrā of the wheel of the sacred Dharma,
One turns the wheel of Dharma.
Through supreme fearlessness one swiftly
Grants fearlessness to all beings.
By making the vajra proud posture firm,
One obtains the bliss of Vajrasattva.
With the vajra hook, in an instant,
One summons even all the tathāgatas.
With the mudrā of the arrow, I
Delight the vajra goddess.
Through vajra joy, all the Victors
Also bestow the words "Excellent!"
Binding the great vajra jewel,
One is empowered by the Teachers.
By binding the vajra sun,
One becomes like the vajra sun.
By raising the vajra banner,
One makes a rain of jewels fall.
By binding the vajra smile,
One swiftly laughs like the buddhas.
By binding the vajra flower,
One thereby sees the vajra dharmas.
If one firmly binds the vajra sword,
That cuts through all suffering.
By binding the vajra wheel,
One turns the wheel of Dharma.
The words of all the buddhas
Are accomplished through vajra recitation.
Through the offering of vajra dance,
Even the buddhas come under control.
By binding the body with the vajra armor,
One obtains the vajra essence.
By binding the vajra fang,
I crush even the vajra.
The vajra fist seizes all,
And finds all the accomplishments of the mudrās.

The vajra holder bestows joy;
Vajramālā, a beautiful form;
Vajragītā, melodious song;
Vajranṛtyā brings under control;
Incense, gladness of mind;
Flowers, the ornaments;
The lamp, great splendor;
Vajragandhā, excellent fragrance;
Vajrāṅkuśa summons;
Vajrapāśa causes to enter;
Vajrasphoṭa binds;
And the vajra bell rings out.

Then the extensive rite for binding the mudrā common to all the mudrās is explained. First of all, one should strike the fingers of the vajra bond at the heart and recite this heart mantra: vajra bandha traṭa. Then all the mudrās one binds come under the control of the vajra of one's own body, speech, and mind.

Then, binding the samaya mudrā of the vajra descent, one recites this heart mantra: a. Then, having descended, it remains close by as a friend.

Then one should recollect the great being of the samaya of the mudrā, reciting this heart mantra: mahā samaya satva aḥ. By that all the mudrās are accomplished. This is the extensive rite for accomplishing all the mudrās.

Then the extensive common rite of accomplishment is explained. First, having bound one's own mudrā, one meditates on oneself as the being of one's own mudrā with this heart mantra: samayo haṃ. Then, having meditated on oneself as the being of one's own mudrā, one blesses oneself with this mantra: samaya satva adhitiṣṭha svamāṃ. Then one should accomplish it. This is the extensive rite of accomplishment.

Then the extensive rite of the siddhis is explained. First, if one wishes to accomplish wealth, this is the heart mantra: artha siddhi. When the mudrā is accomplished by it, great wealth is held to arise. Then, if one wishes to accomplish the vajra, this is the heart mantra: vajra siddhi. By it the vajra will be accomplished just as one wishes. Then, if one wishes for the accomplishment of a vidyādhara, this is the heart mantra: vajra vidyādhara siddhi. By it one accomplishes the state of a vidyādhara just as one wishes. Likewise, if one wishes to accomplish the supreme, one accomplishes it with the heart mantra of one's own mudrā. This is the extensive rite of the siddhis.

Then the extensive common rite, common to all the mudrās, for making one's own body, speech, and mind vajra is explained. Whenever the blessing of the mudrā has grown slack, or if one wishes to give oneself up, one should then make it firm with this heart mantra: oṃ vajra satva samaya manupālaya vajrasatva tvenopa tiṣṭha dṛḍho me bhava sutoṣyo me bhava anurakto me bhava supoṣyo me bhava sarvva siddhi mme prayaccha sarvva karmmasu ca me cittaṃ śreyaḥ kuru hūṃ ha ha ha hoḥ bhagavān sarvva tathāgata vajra māme muñca vajrī bhava mahā samaya satva aḥ. By it, even one who has committed the acts of immediate retribution, deceived all the tathāgatas, reviled the sacred Dharma, and committed every wrong, because Vajrasattva has been made firm by one who practices the mudrās of all the tathāgatas, will swiftly accomplish in this very life all the siddhis just as he wishes: the supreme siddhi, the vajra siddhi, the siddhi of Vajrasattva, up to the siddhi of a tathāgata. So spoke the Blessed One, the Vajrasattva of all the tathāgatas.

Then the extensive common rite for releasing all the mudrās is explained. First, one releases each of the mudrās from the very place where it arose with this heart mantra: oṃ vajra muḥ. Then, with the jewel vajra mudrā that has arisen from the heart and remains at the place of one's own empowerment, one confers empowerment; circling the two index fingers as a garland and binding it, one likewise binds the armor too, with this heart mantra: oṃ vajra ratna abhiṣiñca sarvva mudrā me tridhī kuru vara ka bace na vaṃ. Then, again, having fixed the bound garland at the end of the armor, one strikes the palms together evenly and gives delight with this heart mantra: vajra tuṣya hoḥ. If one releases, binds, and gives delight to the mudrās by this rite, one becomes like a vajra. Or else it may also be done with Vajrasattva.

Reciting Vajrasattva once,
Whatever one wishes, with ease,
Is accomplished by mere recitation,
Just as Vajrapāṇi has spoken.

So spoke the Blessed One Samantabhadra.

For the beings, Vajrasattva and the rest,
For accomplishment and for all actions,
If here one recites as one pleases,
One obtains siddhis from all the ritual systems.
Heart mantras, mudrās, mantras, and vidyās,
In whatever manner one wishes,
Whether explained in the ritual systems or of one's own making,
All of them apply to this accomplishment.

Then, uttering the secret offering and singing the song of vajra praise, one should perform the four secret offerings:

oṃ Through the embrace of Vajrasattva,
The unsurpassed vajra jewel;
Through singing the vajra Dharma,
One performs the vajra action.

Then, in the central maṇḍala, with that very song of vajra praise, one should perform the vajra dance with the hands joined side by side, and make the offerings of incense and the rest. Then, in the outer maṇḍala, one makes offerings with vajra incense and the rest, and places those offerings each in its own place. Then one commands, "All of you, make offerings as best you can!", and, having made request to all the tathāgatas, one has them make offerings of incense and the rest as they wish. One gratifies those who have entered with all the flavors, foods, and so forth that are available, and with all the requisites offered to the great maṇḍala, and then gives them the vajra vow, the siddhi of all the tathāgatas:

This is all the buddhas,
Abiding in the hand of Vajrasattva.
You too must always hold it:
The vow of Vajrapāṇi is firm.

oṃ sarvva tathāgata siddhi vajra samaya tiṣṭha eṣa tvāṃ ddārayāmi vajra satva hi hi hi hi hūṃ

One commands them, "From this day on, do not speak of any of this to anyone," and one also teaches the heart mantra of the oath.

Then one dismisses those who have entered. The request to all the tathāgatas is this: binding the mudrā of the sattva vajra, one shows it facing upward, recites this heart mantra, and releases it:

oṃ You have accomplished every aim of beings;
Grant the siddhis that accord with it.
Please depart to the realm of the buddhas,
And then I pray you to come again.

vajra satva muḥ

One should do the same for all the maṇḍalas. The supreme samaya mudrās too should be released.

This completes the extensive rite of the great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu, from the King of the Realization of the Great Vehicle of All the Tathāgatas.

The third fascicle.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called "the vajra arisen from the samaya of the vajra dhāraṇī of all the tathāgatas." As soon as he had entered into it, from the hearts of all the tathāgatas that very Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, holding the form of Vajradhara, became the goddesses of the samaya mudrās of the vajra dhāraṇī, the all-blazing essence, and came forth. All the tathāgatas of all the buddhas of all the world realms accomplished the gnosis of the vajra dhāraṇī of all the tathāgatas; and they, having become the dhāraṇīs of the samaya mudrās of all the tathāgatas, were arrayed upon moon disks in the manner of the array of the great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu of all the tathāgatas, and uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! Because the mind of awakening
Wishes to benefit all beings,
The hero, by the power of taming them,
Has taken on the form of a woman.

Then the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the Vajradhātu, the samaya of the gnosis mudrā of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ vajra dhātvīśvari hūṃ vajriṇi

Then the Tathāgata Akṣobhya too entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the maṇḍala of the mudrā of the samaya gnosis of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ vajra vajriṇi hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Ratnasaṃbhava entered into the samādhi called "blessed by the maṇḍala of the mudrā of the gnosis of the samaya of the vajra jewel of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ ratna vajriṇi hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Amitāyus entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the maṇḍala of the mudrā of the gnosis of the samaya of the vajra Dharma of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ dharmma vajriṇi hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Amoghasiddhi entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the maṇḍala of the mudrā of the gnosis of the samaya of the vajra action of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ karmma vajriṇi hūṃ

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi explained the four samaya mudrās of the great dhāraṇī of all the tathāgatas: oṃ vajra satva guhya samye ha oṃ guhya ratna aṃkuśi hūṃ oṃ guhya rāgi rāgāya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya vajra sādhvīśvari hūṃ. Samantabhadrā, Tathāgatāṅkuśī, the Joyful Passionate One, and the Excellent One: these are the great vajra dhāraṇīs.

oṃ vajra guhya ratna samaya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya prabhe hūṃ oṃ vajra dhvaja agra guhya hūṃ oṃ vajra hāsa vajri hūṃ. Ratnottamā, Ratnolkā, Dhvajāgrakeyūrā, and Hāsavatī: these are the dhāraṇīs of the vajra jewel.

oṃ vajra dharmma guhya samaye hūṃ oṃ vajra koṣa guhya samaya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya maṇḍale hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya vaca samaye hūṃ. The Vajra Lotus, the All-Holding One, Samantacakrā, and the One Who Echoes Above: these are the dhāraṇīs of the Dharma.

oṃ vajra guhya karmma samaye hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya kapace hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya daṃṣṭā dhāraṇī hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya muṣṭi hūṃ. Siddhottarā, Sarvarakṣā, the One Who Steals Back Radiance, and the Dhāraṇī Mudrā: these are the dhāraṇīs of all.

Then again the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi explained the four samaya mudrās of the vajra secret of all the tathāgatas: oṃ vajra guhya satva vajriṇi hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya ratna vajriṇi hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya dharmma vajriṇi hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya karmma vajriṇi hūṃ. These, the vajra perfections themselves and the rest, are the samaya mudrās that gather the dhāraṇīs of the samaya of the vajra secret of all the tathāgatas. In the great maṇḍala of Vajradhātvīśvarī they are placed, ablaze, seated upon moon disks.

Then Vajrapāṇi explained the four samaya mudrās of the secret offering of all the tathāgatas: oṃ vajra guhya rati būjā samaye sarvva pūjā pravarttanāya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya abhiṣeka pūjā samaye sarvva pūjā pravarttanāya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya gīti pūjā samaye sarvva pūjā pravarttanāya hūṃ oṃ vajra guhya nṛtya pūjā samaye sarvva pūjā pravarttanāya hūṃ. These, the vajra-holding goddess and the rest, ablaze, in the form of the mudrās of their own emblems, are arranged in the corners of the wheel maṇḍala.

Then again Vajrapāṇi explained this great vajra maṇḍala called Vajraguhya:

Next I shall fully explain
The sublime vajra maṇḍala.
Because it resembles the Vajradhātu,
It is called Vajraguhya.

In the manner of the great maṇḍala
All the maṇḍalas should be drawn.
In the center of all the maṇḍalas
One should draw the mudrā of the Buddha.
Seated well upon a stūpa throne,
It is declared to be the Sovereign Lady of the Vajradhātu.
Upon a throne, a vajra laid upon a vajra
Is declared to be Vajrasattva.
Upon the throne of the vajra jewel
Is declared to be the conferring of one's own empowerment.
Upon the throne of the vajra lotus
Is declared to be what is called the weapon.
Upon the throne of the action vajra
Is declared to be what is called the all-vajra.
What is drawn upon the lotus
Rests upon a maṇḍala of light.

Upon the throne draw a vajra;
Likewise two raised hooks;
A vajra resting against a vajra;
Likewise two giving "Excellent!"
The jewel should blaze with rays of light;
Likewise the mudrā of the sun;
The tip of the banner too, with its blaze;
Between two vajras, a row of teeth;
In the center of a vajra draw a lotus;
Likewise a sword with its blaze.

A vajra wheel with vajra spokes;
A blazing tip, full of rays of light;
A vajra with points on every side;
Armor perfectly endowed with vajras;
Likewise draw the vajra fangs;
Make the two hands into the mudrā of fists.
Draw the sattva vajra and the rest.
Just as in the Dhātu maṇḍala,
So is the drawing of the symbol mudrās:
In the maṇḍala, Vajradhara and the rest,
And outside it as well, as is fitting,
Draw their own symbols too.
Maitreya and the others, their own emblems,
Draw just as one pleases.

Next comes the extensive rite of entering this Vajraguhya maṇḍala and so forth. First the vajra master should himself bind the mudrā of Sattvavajrī and enter. Having entered, he circumambulates once, offers that mudrā to the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, releases it properly at his own heart, and at the four gates performs the actions as they should be done with Vajrāṅkuśa and the others; then he comes out. Having come out, he leads the vajra disciples in, in the same manner as leading them into the great Vajradhātu maṇḍala. Then, having led them in, he gives them the secret vajra symbol of accomplishment covered with liquor, and they are to be trained in the wisdom of the Vajraguhya mudrās. Of these, they are first to be trained in the wisdom of the body mudrā of Vajraguhya.

In the center of a moon disk,
Joining hands and feet palm to palm,
Opening the mouth and meditating on a vajra,
One makes even the vajra lady fall in love.
With a hook formed by the bent arms,
One should meditate on a vajra upon the head;
Making an opening with the hand,
One summons even the hook lady.
In the manner of shooting an arrow,
Drawing the bow and loosing it at the heart,
One oneself delights even the lady of vajra joy,
In the manner of Māra.
At the heart of one's own body,
In the manner of vajra armor,
If one binds with arms crossed,
One protects even the buddhas.

Here their heart mantras are these: vajrarāga maho vajra aṃkuśa jaḥ vajra māraya māraya phaṭ vajrabandha rakṣa haṃ

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of the gaze mudrā of Vajraguhya.

With the passion of the vajra gaze,
Opening the eyes wide with joy,
If one looks at a woman thereby,
She always comes under one's power.
Moving the eyes very swiftly
And summoning with the eyelashes,
This is declared to be the radiant gaze;
It summons all beings.
Drawing the wrathful frown tightly together,
The wrathful eyes are narrowed;
If one makes the wrathful gaze,
One terrifies even the three realms.
Like a crag of Mount Meru, king of mountains,
Steady, with eyes that do not blink:
This is declared to be the loving gaze;
It dispels disease, grahas, and poison.

Then their heart mantras are these: vajradṛṣṭi maṭ dīptadṛṣṭi aṃkuśa jaḥ krodhadṛṣṭi hrīḥ dṛḍha tiṣṭha phaṭ

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of the speech mudrā of Vajraguhya.

If one says ho ho ho ho,
Being free of sound, at once
It delights all sentient beings;
That is the bestowal of the vajra word.
If one utters jja jja jja jja,
The wrathful word is clear;
It summons all people,
Even those who are like Vajradhara.
If one utters hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ,
Through the clarity of what is spoken,
It becomes a māra to all sentient beings,
Even to those who are like Mount Meru.
If one says haṃ haṃ haṃ haṃ,
The subtle word is clear;
It protects those of the world,
And protects even the Victor whose nature is vajra.

Here the heart mantras are these: vajra hoḥ vajra jaḥ vajra hūṃ vajra haṃ

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of the mind mudrā of Vajraguhya.

If one meditates on oneself
As you, Vajrapāṇi,
Endowed with the supreme of all aspects,
One brings all the buddhas under one's power.
If one meditates on oneself
As Vajragarbha himself,
Endowed with the supreme of all aspects,
One summons Vajrapāṇi.
If one meditates on oneself
As the very Dharma Eye,
Endowed with the supreme of all aspects,
One thereby gathers all phenomena.
If one meditates on oneself
As Vajraviśva himself,
Endowed with the supreme of all aspects,
One thereby protects all the vajra ones.

Here the heart mantras are these: vajrabāṇi sarvva śamānaya sarvva buddha ho vajragarbha vajrapāṇi śīghraṃ ākarṣaya hūṃ jaḥ vajranetra sarvva dharmma māraya hūṃ phaḍ vajraviśva rakṣa sarbhān hūṃ

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of the Vajraguhya mudrās.

At the heart of one's own body,
Bind the sattva vajra,
And if one looks with the vajra eye,
One brings down all beings.
At the heart of one's own body,
Bind the jewel vajra,
And if one looks with the blazing eye,
One brings all under one's power.
At the heart of one's own body,
If one binds the Dharma vajra
And looks with the wrathful eye,
One thereby gathers all beings.
At the heart of one's own body,
Bind the action vajra,
And if one looks with the loving eye,
One protects all beings.

Then these are the heart mantras of those Vajraguhya wisdom mudrās: vajraguhyasamaya hūṃ vajraguhyasamya haṃ vajraguhyasamaya hūṃ vajraguhya samya haṃ

Then one is to be trained in binding the great mudrās of Vajraguhya.

What arises from the vajra joining of palms
Is declared to be the great secret.
The great mudrās, in brief:
Their binding is explained.
Crossing the two thumbs,
And bending the two index fingers,
Make the middle fingers even, joined at the joints:
This is declared to be the Sovereign Lady of the Vajradhātu.
From that same one, make the middle fingers a vajra;
Make the two middle fingers a jewel;
For the lotus, the ring, little, and middle fingers,
With the fingers of the hand fully stretched out.
For the index finger vajra, the tips of the two index fingers,
That same one adorned by the two thumbs.
Giving "Excellent!", and the index finger jewel;
The index finger jewel is made to blaze.
The ring fingers side by side, the little finger jewel;
That same one is also turned around.
At the tips of all, spread them fully apart;
Insert the thumbs and little fingers well;
The fingers fully stretched out as a wheel;
That same one is drawn forth from the mouth.
Extending the thumbs, cover the vajra;
Making the index fingers even, insert them inside;
From that very one, separating the two little fingers,
Then inside, make a vajra.
The actions of Vajraguhya
Arise from the vajra binding.
The Dharma secret ones, with their signs,
Are made with the palms bent inward.

The action secret ones, by the signs abiding within,
Are shown as the actions.
Then further, it is fully explained:
The Dharma mudrās, explained in brief:
a ja ho sa oṃ oṃ trāṃ ha hrīḥ dhaṃ maṃ raṃ kaṃ haṃ hūṃ vaṃ
Having made the secret mudrās twofold,
Make them into the action mudrās.
However many supreme samayas there are,
Separate them into two, and do likewise.

Then their accomplishment is explained.

Saying samaya stvaṃ,
What is accomplished by binding them oneself
Is the self of bliss, attached to desire.
Of these, not even a single one, above all,
Should be bound at all times;
For a secret, or for a great purpose,
The wise should apply them.
The self of all of these abides;
The goddesses of the self-arisen ones are steadfast;
Through one's own passion for the accomplishments,
The eternal lord is sent forth.

Then come the actions of binding and so forth for all these mudrās.

Whether for oneself or for another,
When one performs the Vajrāveśa, the vajra possession,
This is the heart mantra to employ,
Whether one binds or causes another to bind:
vajra hūṃ bandha

Then comes the release.

In brief, all the mudrās,
By this very heart mantra,
From whatever place each one arose,
Should be released into that same place:
oṃ vajra muḥ

Then comes the making firm.

Making firm the jewel vajra,
Release it at the place of the heart and at the crown of the head;
With the index fingers bind the armor;
This is the heart mantra to employ:
oṃ dṛḍta vajra kavaco dṛḍha

Then comes the samaya of binding.

Release them into the places where they abide;
Make them firm with the armor;
By clapping the palms, join them all;
This is the heart mantra to employ:
oṃ guhya samaya tra
Recite Vajrasattva as you wish;
In the maṇḍala and in all actions
Apply it at that very time;
It is supreme for accomplishing everything.

This completes the extensive rite of the Vajraguhya mudrā maṇḍala, from the Great King of Ritual Manuals, the Realization of the Great Vehicle of All the Tathāgatas.

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya maṇḍala of the subtle vajra wisdom mudrā of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ sūkṣma vajra jñāna samaye hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Akṣobhya entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya maṇḍala of the subtle wisdom of the Vajrasattva of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ vajrasatva sūkṣma vajra jñāna samaye hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Ratnasaṃbhava entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya maṇḍala of the subtle wisdom of the vajra jewel of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ ratna sūkṣma vajra jñāna samaya hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Amitāyus entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya maṇḍala of the subtle wisdom of the vajra Dharma of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃdharmma sūkṣma vajra jñānasamaye hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Amoghasiddhi entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya maṇḍala of the subtle wisdom of the vajra action of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ karmma stukṣma vajra jñāna samaye hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata, the Blessed One Vairocana, entered into the samādhi called "the subtle wisdom vajra of all the tathāgatas." The very moment the Blessed One had entered into it, at that very time, those rays of light of subtle vajra wisdom came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas and illuminated all the world systems. They made firm for all sentient beings the equipoise of all the tathāgatas in the samādhi of subtle vajra wisdom. Then, becoming one again, they were focused into a vajra body of the wisdom of samādhi; gathered into one, they became the wisdom of the Tathāgata and entered the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana.

Then the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, having entered the wisdom heart of all the tathāgatas, spoke this great samaya vajra of the subtle wisdom of all the tathāgatas: sūkma vajra

The very moment he had spoken it, that same Blessed One Vajrapāṇi came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, blessed himself into the form of the subtle wisdom vajra of all the tathāgatas, and, abiding at the tip of the noses of all the tathāgatas, uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the subtle vajra
Of all the buddhas, and I am great.
Because, though subtle, I am great,
I fill even the three realms."

When he had spoken thus, the Blessed One, the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, from the tips of the noses of all the tathāgatas, in the manner of expanding the signs of extremely subtle wisdom, pervaded the bodies of all the tathāgatas; and in the manner of pervading the entire dharmadhātu, he pervaded the entire expanse of space with the signs of subtle vajra wisdom. Having pervaded the entire expanse of space, he remained there, having blessed himself into the form of the wisdom vajra of all the tathāgatas.

Then, at that very time, all the tathāgatas focused on the vajra dharmatā at the center of the wisdom vajra of all the tathāgatas, and entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the subtle vajra wisdom of all the tathāgatas." Then from that vajra of all the tathāgatas there emerged this heart of the samādhi wisdom of all the tathāgatas: vajra nābī tathāgatā hūṃ

The very moment it had emerged, the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, in the manner in which subtle wisdom enters, entered the bodies of all the tathāgatas, and, as their heart essence, took the form of a vajra and abided there.

Then from that vajra of all the tathāgatas there emerged these four hearts of great wisdom: vajra ātmaka dṛḍḍha vajra aṃkuśa tiṣṭharāga vajra prabeśa hṛdayaṃ a ho vajra duṣṭi

The wisdom mudrā of Vajrasattva, the wisdom mudrā of the blessing that gathers all the tathāgatas, the wisdom mudrā that delights all the tathāgatas, and the wisdom mudrā of great joy: these are the great samādhis of all the tathāgatas.

Then Vajrapāṇi, the heart of all the tathāgatas, in the manner in which subtle wisdom enters, entered his own heart and remained there, having blessed himself into the form of the heart vajra. Then from that vajra form there emerged these four hearts: vajra ratna ātmaka hṛdaya vajra sūrya tiṣṭha vajra dhājāgra baṃ hṛdaya vajra hāsa

The wisdom mudrā of the vajra empowerment of all the tathāgatas, the wisdom mudrā of the ornament of the maṇḍala of great light, the wisdom mudrā that fulfills the hopes of all the tathāgatas, and the wisdom mudrā from which the force of the joy of all the tathāgatas arises: these are the jewel samādhi of all the tathāgatas.

Then again Vajrapāṇi, in the manner in which subtle wisdom enters, entered the vajra heart at his own heart and remained there, having blessed himself into the form of a vajra. Then from that vajra form there emerged these four hearts: vajra padma ātmaka hṛdya vajra koṣa tiṣṭha vajra cadakra hṛdyaṃ prabeśa vajra jitvāgra hṛdaya

The wisdom mudrā of the sameness of all phenomena, the wisdom mudrā of the discernment of all the tathāgatas, the wisdom mudrā of entering the great wheel, and the wisdom mudrā of the Dharma words of all the tathāgatas, free of elaboration: these are the Dharma samādhi of all the tathāgatas.

Then again Vajrapāṇi came forth from the vajra at the heart, his own heart vajra, and, in the manner of entering subtle wisdom, entered that vajra heart and remained there, having blessed himself into the form of an extremely subtle vajra. Then from that subtle vajra form there emerged these four hearts: sarvva vajra ātmaka hṛdaya vajra kavaca tiṣṣa vajra rakṣa hṛdaya vajra muṣṭi hṛdaya

The wisdom mudrā of the action of all the tathāgatas, the wisdom mudrā of invincible diligence, the wisdom mudrā that destroys the maṇḍalas of all the māras, and the wisdom mudrā that binds all the tathāgatas: these are the action samādhi of all the tathāgatas.

Moreover, the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, in the manner of pervading with the signs of subtle wisdom, came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas and became the body of the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi. Then again he became the bodies of the bodhisattvas Vajrasattva and the rest. Each placed his own hand emblem at his heart, and, in the manner of arranging the great Vajradhātu maṇḍala, they abided upon moon disks and remained settled in equipoise in the samādhi of their own hearts.

Then again Vajrapāṇi, in order to accomplish the samādhi, the wisdom, and the higher knowledges of all the tathāgatas, explained this subtle vajra wisdom maṇḍala.

Next, what is to be fully explained
Is the sublime Dharma maṇḍala.
Because it resembles the Vajradhātu,
It is called the Subtle Vajra.
Then, in the manner of the maṇḍala,
Place the great beings as well.
In the center of the vajra draw the Buddha;
In the buddha maṇḍalas too
Draw the great beings;
Likewise, at their hearts, their own mudrās,
Abiding in samādhi,
The two hands bound in the vajra binding.

Then comes the extensive rite of summoning and so forth for this subtle vajra Dharma maṇḍala. Having performed extensively the rite of entering and so forth in the manner of the great maṇḍala, one places the wisdom mudrā in the disciple's hand, and they are to be trained in the wisdom of the great mudrā that makes one's own mind workable.

Pressing the tongue against the palate,
One should contemplate at the tip of the nose.
Through touching the bliss of the subtle vajra,
The mind becomes settled in equipoise.
If the sign of touching
The bliss of the subtle vajra arises,
That sign is to be made to pervade;
The mind should be made to pervade everywhere.
When the mind has pervaded as far as one wishes,
It will pervade even the three realms.
Then, as for withdrawing it again:
As far as the tip of the nose,
Settle them in perfect equipoise.
Whatever one meditates on,
All of it becomes stable.
This is the testing of samādhi and discernment.

Then their heart mantras are these: sūkṣma vajra spharṇa saṃharaṇa vajra vajra hṛda tiṣṭha

The being who, with great steadfastness,
Has made loving-kindness even,
In the manner in which the mind pervades,
Pervades all sentient beings with it.
In the manner of pervading with loving-kindness,
Whoever is compassionate
Should pervade with a practice
Endowed with benefit for all sentient beings.
All things are by nature luminous,
Pure from the beginning, like space;
Whether Dharma or not Dharma,
Pervading them with intention, one becomes joyful.
Those hard to tame and their like
Are not vessels for a buddha's awakening;
For the sake of understanding them,
One should meditate on great equanimity.

Here the heart mantras are these: mahā maitri spharṇa mahā karuṇā spharaṇa sarvva buddhopramoda spharaṇa sarvva satpaṃ saṃbodhaya

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of recollecting all the tathāgatas.

In space, or in another direction,
In the manner of the subtle vajra,
Whether standing or sitting,
One should cultivate the form of the vajra.
Likewise, in all places,
Through the yoga of the subtle vajra,
Settled in equipoise, at the heart
Meditate on the bodhisattva vajra.
In the sequence just described,
In the manner of the subtle vajra,
The great form of Vajrapāṇi
Should be meditated on in all places.
The buddha body endowed
With the supreme of all aspects, at all times,
In its proper order, just as it is,
Should be meditated on in equipoise.

Here the heart mantras are these:

vajra mukhi ghaga mahā bodhi satva ābeśa vajra paṇi dhārśaya svarūpaṃ buddha anusmṛti ābeśa

In the manner of the subtle vajra,
What one should cultivate oneself
Is oneself in the form of the moon:
This is the meditation of the awakening mind.
In the center of the moon disk
One should meditate on oneself
As having become the form of a vajra:
This is the meditation of the sattva vajra.
Applying the manner of the subtle vajra,
One should meditate on oneself
As Vajrasattva at one's own heart:
This is the meditation of Vajrasattva.
One should meditate on oneself
As the form of a buddha
Endowed with the supreme of all aspects:
This is the meditation of a buddha's awakening.

Here the heart mantras are these: samanta btadra ābeśa vajra satva vajra ābeśa vajrasatva samādhi jñāna ābeśa tathāgato hūṃ

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of the Dharma secret mudrās of all the tathāgatas.

"I am equal to the Tathāgata":
Uttering this once in vajra words,
And joining the two organs in union,
One makes all sentient beings impassioned.
"I am equal to the Great Vajra":
Uttering this once in vajra words,
And joining the two organs in union,
One unfailingly summons the world.
"I myself am equal to Vajradharma":
Uttering this once in vajra words,
By joining the two organs in union,
One thereby destroys the whole world.
"I am equal to the vajra, without remainder":
Uttering this once in vajra words,
By joining the two organs in union,
One thereby accomplishes all actions.

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of the samādhi mudrā called "the blessing of the wisdom vajra of all the tathāgatas."

In the manner of the subtle vajra,
One should meditate, in the center of the vajra,
On oneself as the very body of the Buddha;
Thereby one attains buddhahood itself.

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of the samādhi mudrās of Vajrasattva and the rest.

Applying the manner of the subtle vajra,
With the vajra and the others set at the heart,
Having meditated, through the vajra and the rest
One bestows one's own accomplishment.

Then one is to be trained in the wisdom of the samaya mudrās of the samādhi of the families of all the tathāgatas.

Arising from the vajra binding,
They are explained as sixteen;
Samādhi is the supreme samaya;
Their binding is explained.
Sitting cross-legged, lying on the back,
The right ten likewise inserted back again,
Resting at the heart is the fourth:
This is the maṇḍala of Vajrasattva and the rest.
Placed at the forehead, and at the nape of the neck,
And at the shoulders, and joined with laughter;
Placed at the mouth, at the heart the sword,
Spread at the heart, and drawn forth from the mouth;
At the crown, the seeing, placed at the mouth;
The two index fingers bent, likewise in front.

Then further one should train in the Dharma mudrās: ṭakki gra gra maṭa agra trāṃ trāṃ aṃ aṃ baṃ baṃ tiṭa hīḥṭa bhṛṭa kraśa ha ha ba ba baṃ baṃ phaṭ grasa

Then the supreme of the subtle wisdoms of Dharma action is explained.

From the samaya of the wisdom fist,
Making it twofold, one should apply them.

This completes the extensive rite of the vajra wisdom Dharma maṇḍala, from the King of the Great Realization of the Great Vehicle of All the Tathāgatas.

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya wisdom of action that pervades with the extensive rite of the unsurpassed offering of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ sarvva tathāgata vajra dhātu anuttara pūja karmma spharaṇa samye hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Akṣobhya entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya vajra of action that pervades with the extensive rite of the unsurpassed offering of the Vajrasattva of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ sarvva tathāgata vajrasatva anuttara būjā karmma spharṇa samye hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Ratnasaṃbhava entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya vajra of action that pervades with the extensive rite of the unsurpassed offering of the vajra jewel of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ sarvva tathāgata vajra ratna anuttara pūjā karmma spharaṇa samaye hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Amitāyus entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya of action that pervades with the extensive rite of the unsurpassed offering of the vajra Dharma of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ sarvva tathagata vajra dharmma anuttara pūjā karmma spharaṇa samye hūṃ

Then the Tathāgata Amoghasiddhi entered into the samādhi called "the blessing of the samaya vajra of action that pervades with the extensive rite of the unsurpassed offering of the vajra action of all the tathāgatas," and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ sarvva tathāgata vajra karmma anuttara pūjā karmma spharaṇa samaye hūṃ

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana entered into the samādhi called "the samaya vajra of action that pervades the entire dharmadhātu with the extensive rite of offering of all the tathāgatas." The very moment he had entered into it, that same Blessed One Vajrapāṇi came forth from the hearts of all the tathāgatas, having become goddesses: oceans of clouds of the extensive rite of the ornaments of every kind of offering, of many varieties, filling the whole expanse of space and pervading the entire dharmadhātu with the extensive rite of offering of all the tathāgatas. Then, as oceans of clouds throughout the boundless world systems, in the maṇḍalas of the retinues of all the tathāgatas, they brought about the generation of the great, unsurpassed awakening mind of all the tathāgatas, the delighting of the families of all the tathāgatas, the accomplishment of the conduct of Samantabhadra, the approach to the seat of great awakening, the taming of all the māras, the full and perfect awakening to the sameness of all the tathāgatas, and

the generation of the great maṇḍala of all the tathāgatas, the gaining of complete victory over all the three realms, the turning of the wheel of the sublime Dharma, and the acting for the benefit of the realms of sentient beings, all of them without exception or remainder. Having displayed all these and the other miraculous powers and magical displays of the buddhas, they remained there.

Those goddesses of the oceans of clouds of offerings, too, holding their own mudrās with both hands, made offering to all the tathāgatas according to the rite. Abiding upon moon disks in the manner of the great Vajradhātu maṇḍala, they uttered this inspired utterance:

"How wonderful! I am the offering to the buddhas;
I am the one who performs all offerings.
For, endowed with the blessing of the buddhas,
I bring all the buddhas to accomplishment."

Then Vajrapāṇi explained this action maṇḍala called Vajra, the extensive rite of the actions of offering and so forth to all the tathāgatas:

oṃ sarvva tathāgata sarvva ātmānana ryitrana pūjā spharaṇa karmma vajriṇi hūṃ ā oṃ sarvva tathāgata sarvva ātān niryātna pūjā spharaṇa karmma gri jaḥ oṃ sarvva tathāgata sarvva ātmānana ryītana anurāgapūjā spharaṇa karmma pāṇe hūṃ ho oṃ sarvva tathāgata sarvva ātmān niryātana sādhukār pūjā spharaṇa karmma tuṣṭi aḥ

The blissful lady of the supreme joy of all the tathāgatas, the lady who invites all the tathāgatas, the lady who pleases all the tathāgatas, and the lady who delights all the tathāgatas: these are the great offering of all the tathāgatas.

oṃ namaḥ sarvva tathāgata kāyābhiṣeka ratnebhyo vajra maṇi oṃ oṃ namaḥ sarba tathāgata sūryebhyo vajra tejeni jvāla hrīḥ oṃ namaḥ sarvva tathāgatāśā paripūraṇi cindāmaṇi dhvaja agrebhyo vajra dhvaja agri trāṃ oṃ namaḥ sarvva tathāgata mahā prīti pramodya karebhyo vajra hāsye ha

The great lady of empowerment, the lady of great illumination, the lady who rains down great jewels, and the lady of supreme joy and delight: these are the empowerment offering of all the tathāgatas.

oṃ sarvva tathāgata vajra dharmatā samādhibhi stunomi mahā dharmāgri hrīḥ oṃ sarvva tathāgata prajñāpāramitā nirhāraiḥ stunomi mahā ghoṣānuge dhaṃ oṃ sarvva tathāgata cakra akṣaraṃ paribarthādi sarba sūtrāntanyaiḥ stunomi sarba maṇḍale hūṃ oṃ sarba tathāgata sandhi bhāṣa buddha sadgīti bhirga yaṃ stunomi vajra bāce baṃ

The lady who sings of great wisdom, the lady who follows the great melody, the lady who enters all maṇḍalas, and the lady of mantra conduct: these are the Dharma offering of all the tathāgatas.

oṃ sarba tathāgata dhupa megha spharaṇa pūjā karme karakra oṃ sarba tathāgata pūṣba prasara spharaṇa pūjā karme kiri kira oṃ sarba tathāgata āloka jvala spharaṇa pūjā karme bhara bhara oṃ sarba tathāgata gandha samudrā spharaṇa pūjā karma kuru kuru

The fragrant lady, the lady endowed with the limbs of great awakening, the lady endowed with eyes, and the lady endowed with scent: these are the action offering of all the tathāgatas.

Then the Vajra Activity, the action maṇḍala for making offering to all the tathāgatas without exception or remainder, is explained.

Next, what is to be fully explained
Is the sublime action maṇḍala.
Because it resembles the Vajradhātu,
It is also known as Vajra Activity.
In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
Set out the bodies of the buddhas;
In the manner of Vajrasattva and the rest,
Draw the goddesses together with their mudrās.

Then this extensive rite of leading disciples into this action maṇḍala, Vajra Activity, and so forth is explained. First one should lead them in, in the manner of the great Vajradhātu maṇḍala. Having led them in, one should instruct them thus: "This is the samaya of offering to all the tathāgatas; therefore, each and every day, perform these sixteen offerings as best you can." Then one removes the face covering, shows them the action maṇḍala, and gives the symbols of all of them into their two hands. Then, since even all the tathāgatas make offering to such a one, what need is there to speak of others?

Then one should train in the wisdom of the offering mudrās that accomplish the great awakening mind.

Through firmly generating the awakening mind,
Thinking, "I am the Buddha,"
I, as joy, make offering,
And attain even the bliss of the buddhas.
Through firmly generating the awakening mind,
Thinking, "I am the Buddha,"
If one makes offering with offerings such as garlands,
One is thereby empowered as well.
Through firmly generating the awakening mind,
Thinking, "I am the Buddha,"
When I make offering with sweet songs,
That offering becomes delight.
Through firmly generating the awakening mind,
Thinking, "I am the Buddha,"
When I make offering with the offering of dance,
The buddhas, too, will make offering to me.

Here the heart mantras are these: buddha ātmako'taṃ buddha abhiṣiñcami buddha stuti karomi buddha pūjā karomi

Then one should train in the wisdom of the mudrās of offering to all the buddhas.

Prostrating in the supreme manner
Of the vajra of body, speech, and mind,
If one likewise makes offering to all the buddhas,
One becomes forever worthy of homage.
All the buddhas, of great merit,
Their body, speech, and mind born of vajra:
If one makes offering to them by rejoicing,
One will swiftly attain buddhahood.
With all the offerings, such as offering oneself
As divine substance of the vajra of body, speech, and mind,
If one makes offering to the buddhas,
One also becomes an object of offering.
All the accumulations of virtue
Of the vajra of body, speech, and mind,
Through the offering of complete dedication,
Make one equal to all the buddhas.

Here the heart mantras are these: praṇāmāmi anumode buddha pūjā pañcama

Then one should train in the wisdom of the mudrās of Dharma offering.

Phenomena are luminous by their intrinsic nature as well;
By their essential nature they are pure from the very beginning.
By making offering with those dharmas,
One will attain the bliss of joy.
Of all phenomena gathered together,
The syllable a is declared to be the gateway.
Through that Dharma mudrā,
All of one's suffering is cut off.
Here the cause of all phenomena
Is the Tathāgata.
Through the offering of the wheel of the sublime Dharma,
By making offering, one will come to expound the Dharma.
All phenomena, by their intrinsic nature,
Are said to be like melodious sounds;
If one makes offering with those Dharma offerings,
One also attains the speech of the Buddha.

Here their heart mantras are these: sarva śuddha samanta bhadra dharma cakra niṣpra pañca

Then one should train in the wisdom of the mudrās of the offering of meditative absorption.

The subtle particles of one's own
Vajra of body, speech, and mind:
If one meditates on them as the Vajradhātu,
One swiftly attains the vajra nature.
The subtle particles of one's own
Vajra of body, speech, and mind:
If one meditates on them as all the buddhas,
One swiftly achieves the Dharma body.
The subtle particles of one's own
Vajra of body, speech, and mind:
If one meditates on them as Vajrasattva,
One becomes equal to Vajrasattva.
The subtle particles of one's own
Vajra of body, speech, and mind:
If one meditates on them as the body of the Buddha,
One becomes a perfectly awakened buddha.

Here their heart mantras are these: vajra kāya dharma kāya satva kāya buddha kāya

Then one should train in the wisdom of the mudrās of secret offering.

The offering of the bliss
Of embracing the Self-Arisen One with the whole body:
If one presents it, one will swiftly
Become like Vajrasattva.
The bliss of sublime holding
In the union of intense passion:
If it is presented to the buddhas,
One becomes like Vajraratna.
The supreme bliss of union
Brought about through great delight and blissful passion:
If it is presented to the buddhas,
One becomes like Vajradharma.
The bliss of complete union
In the joining of the two organs:
By presenting it as an offering,
One becomes like Vajrakarma.

Their secret heart mantras are these: rati vajra rāga vajra prati vajra karma vajra

Then one should train in the wisdom mudrā of the great mudrā of the activity of offering of all the tathāgatas:

Joining them at the heart, the sides, and the back,
And likewise at the forehead and so on,
The mouth, the ears, and the nape of the neck,
One places them at the crown, the shoulders, and the waist.

One should train in the wisdom of the samaya offering mudrās of all the tathāgatas:

In the manner of the great mudrā,
Make the vajra binding firm,
Place it in the manner of the sites, at the heart and so on,
And then make offering to the Victors.

Then one should train in the wisdom of the Dharma mudrās of offering to all the tathāgatas: oṃ kra sayya sā dṛ raṃ kraṃ khaṃ śaṃ ha hūṃ hūṃ śa no śīsāṃ

To summarize the karma mudrās,
The karma mudrā is to be made twofold.

From the Great Vehicle Realization of All the Tathāgatas, the extensive maṇḍala rite of the great ritual king, called Vajrakarma, is complete.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, generated the family of all the tathāgatas, and in order to gather all the accomplishments of this extensive rite of the great ritual king of the family of all the tathāgatas, he spoke this heart mantra of the mudrā of all the tathāgatas: oṃ sarva tathāgata muṣṭi vaṃ

Then, again, the Tathāgata Akṣobhya, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, generated the family of all the tathāgatas, and in order to gather all the accomplishments of this extensive rite of the great ritual king of the family of all the tathāgatas, he spoke this heart mantra of the mudrā of all the tathāgatas: oṃ vajra satva muṣṭi a

Then the Tathāgata Ratnasaṃbhava, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, generated the family of all the tathāgatas, and in order to gather all the accomplishments of this extensive rite of the great ritual king of the family of all the tathāgatas, he spoke this heart mantra of the mudrā of all the tathāgatas: oṃ vajra ratna muṣṭi trāṃ

Then the Tathāgata Amitāyus, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, generated the family of all the tathāgatas, and in order to gather all the accomplishments of this extensive rite of the great ritual king of all the tathāgatas, he spoke this heart mantra of the mudrā of all the tathāgatas: oṃ vajra dharma muṣṭi khaṃ
