If one recites the vajra words a hundred times, one will swiftly attain accomplishment. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

If one wishes for the unsurpassed accomplishment, then on a cloth the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, is seated in the center, and his four great sattvas are arranged in the manner of a maṇḍala, with colors and ornaments as one pleases. One draws them, with the great sattvas seated on moon discs, and one draws the goddesses of the family in the corners. Then, having made extensive offerings to the painted image, one should recite for four months in the proper manner of recitation. Then at the end, having made extensive offerings, one should recite throughout the whole night. Then, when dawn breaks, one will attain the supreme accomplishments, such as the reality of all the tathāgatas. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

Or, if one wishes to accomplish the mudrā, one binds the mudrā of Sattvavajrī of all the tathāgatas, and before the eyes of the mudrā of the painted image, binding and releasing it in the proper way as one pleases, one should recite the vajra words a hundred thousand times. Then at the end, throughout the whole night, without wearying, one releases the mudrā only slightly, binds it again in the proper way, and recites. Then the mudrā will blaze, descend, leap up, and speak a few words. Then, with the mudrā still bound, one will soar in the sky. One will also take on whatever form one pleases, become invisible, and perform every action. As soon as they are bound, all the mudrās too will perform every action in the proper way. So spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

Or, if one wishes to accomplish absorption, one begins with the subtle vajra and meditates for four months in the manner of the yoga of the heart of whichever absorption one delights in. Then at the end, throughout the whole night, one should remain in meditative equipoise without breaking the cross-legged posture. Then, when dawn breaks, all the accomplishments, such as that of the tathāgata, will become directly manifest. Then one will obtain whatever one desires. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

If one wishes to accomplish action, one likewise recites and practices for one month, and then at the end one should recite throughout one whole night. Then one will have the accomplishment of all actions. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

If one wishes to perform some act, merely by uttering it once, oneself and others, villages and towns, will be protected by binding on armor and the like. One may also perform the rite of descent: with fragrant sandalwood, those seized by grahas and those not seized, and all objects, such as the mudrās of the vajra, hook, arrow, hand, jewel, sun, banner, row of teeth, lotus, sword, wheel, tongue, crossed vajra, armor, fangs and fist, one causes to descend by a single utterance.

One also performs the acts of illusion. One places a vajra inside a peacock tail feather and binds it, then presses that peacock feather with the mudrā of Sattvavajrī, and should recite until the mudrā of Sattvavajrī of all the tathāgatas descends. Then various forms and so forth will appear from the peacock feather. From then on, the peacock feather, according to the rite, displays every form on one's own body; reciting once over that very feather and waving it, every form will appear from oneself. With that very feather one also displays the illusory acts of the world, and displays the images of the buddhas and bodhisattvas. It displays the deeds performed in all the buddha fields of the ten directions by the tathāgatas together with the maṇḍalas of their retinues, such as the taming of the māras with their hosts and the play of miraculous powers, up to displaying from oneself the image of the Buddha endowed with all the supreme aspects.

If one wishes to bring under one's power, one binds the mudrā of the vajrī of the mind of all the tathāgatas and should recite until that mudrā blazes. From then on, if merely by binding the mudrā one pleases all the tathāgatas and brings them under one's power, what need is there to mention other sattvas?

If one wishes to accomplish the worldly and transcendent accomplishments, then first of all one likewise recites, reciting eight thousand times for one month. Then at the end one binds the mudrā and likewise should recite throughout the whole night until the mudrā blazes. From then on, merely by binding the mudrā, any one worldly accomplishment whatsoever will come about. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

The extensive requisites and rite of the tathāgata family are complete.

Then this is the extensive requisites and rite of the vajra family. First one should perform the preliminary approach: in this way, as one wishes, one should recite the syllables a hundred thousand times. At its end, having recited throughout the whole night, from then on one will be able to benefit all sentient beings and to punish them. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

But if one wishes to accomplish it, one should draw the painted image in the proper way, and likewise make offerings. Then for one month, enjoying every pleasure and doing as one pleases, one recites with the vajra words at the four times of day so as to complete a hundred thousand recitations. When the month has come to its end, one makes extensive offerings to the painted image, binds the mudrā called Vajra Hūṃ, and should recite until the sound hūṃ arises from that bound mudrā. Then, when dawn breaks, the lord of the gods, Maheśvara (Śiva), and the others, together with their retinues, will stand before one and receive one's command.

Then the vidyādhara should command them thus: "Whenever I command you, 'Come here and do this!', at that time you must come here and do as I command."

From then on one becomes the master of the whole of the three worlds. Even in a moment one gives commands as one pleases throughout all the three realms and travels round them, and on returning one also benefits and punishes. Enjoying the three worlds of divine substances, one does as one pleases, and one delights all women, such as the consorts of the lords of all the gods, and enjoys them; yet no one at all, whoever he may be, is able to harm one. With hūṃ one subdues all the malevolent ones, gods and others, and then one will live for a thousand minor eons. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

If one wishes to accomplish the mudrā, one performs recitation in the proper way, and at the end of the recitation one binds the commitment mudrā called Vajra Hūṃ and should recite throughout the whole night. Then, when dawn breaks, the mudrā will be accomplished. Then, if one binds the mudrā together with hūṃ in accordance with the proper rite, one will perform every action: going through the sky, displaying various forms, becoming invisible, displaying illusions, causing descent upon all, summoning, bringing under one's control, producing joy in all, seizing all jewels, displaying great blazing light rays, displaying a rain of jewels, laughing a great laugh, purifying all sentient beings, cutting, dividing, turning the wheel of time, declaring reality as it is, engaging in all actions, protecting, stupefying, subduing, threatening, killing, sealing all sentient beings, engaging in the joy of desire, empowering, making all things sing, making them dance, summoning, causing to enter, binding, causing descent, and so forth. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

If one wishes to accomplish the subtle vajra, one meditates in the absorption called Vajra Hūṃ, and likewise should practice for one month in the manner of recitation. Then at the end, with that same absorption, one should recite throughout one whole night. Then, when dawn breaks, one comes to have the five superknowledges and will be able to benefit all sentient beings and to subjugate them. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

If one wishes to accomplish action, one likewise recites and practices for one month, and then at the end should recite throughout one whole night. Then all actions will be accomplished. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

Then, if one wishes to perform the actions, one utters it once, and by thinking of it in the mind one will perform all actions, such as protection.

The extensive requisites and rite of the vajra family are complete.

Then this is the extensive requisites and rite of the lotus family. First, if one performs a hundred thousand recitations, the approach will have been performed. Then one should practice the accomplishment: on a cloth one draws the Blessed One, Tamer of All Beings, and the rest; in all directions around him one draws the four great sattvas in the manner of four maṇḍalas, and in the corners one draws the goddesses. Then one performs the rite of recitation extensively in the same way, and at the end of the month one should recite throughout one whole night. Then the Blessed One, Tamer of All Beings, will come and, at the appropriate time, will grant the supreme opportunity. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

If one wishes to accomplish the mudrā, one likewise binds the mudrā of Padmavajrī and should recite at the four times of day until a full eight thousand recitations have been spoken. Then at the end, throughout one whole night, one binds the mudrā and recites, and when dawn breaks there will be accomplishment. Then, by binding the mudrā, one tames all beings as is fitting. Thus spoke the Blessed One Samantāvalokiteśvara.

If one wishes to accomplish it by way of absorption, one likewise, at the end of the month, should recite throughout one whole night with whatever absorption one delights in. Then, when dawn breaks, all absorptions will become directly manifest. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

If one wishes to accomplish action, one likewise recites, and at the end of the month should recite throughout the whole night. Then one will be capable of all actions. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

The extensive requisites and rite of the lotus family are complete.

Then this is the extensive rite of application of the jewel family. First of all, one performs the four kinds of homage to all the tathāgatas and should recite a hundred thousand times. Then likewise one draws on a cloth the Blessed One Sarvārthasiddhi in the manner of the four maṇḍalas. Then one practices in the same way, and at the end of the month should recite throughout one whole night. Then, when dawn breaks, the jewel of the empowerment of all the blessed tathāgatas, the bodhisattva Ākāśagarbha, will come and bestow empowerment. By that empowerment, one will become a wheel-turning sovereign of the vidyādharas in that great trichiliocosm world system. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

If one wishes to accomplish the mudrā, then likewise, in the manner of the Dharma Vajrī, one should accomplish the mudrā of the sprout of Vajraratna just as it is; then one will perform the works of every intention. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

If one wishes to accomplish jewel wisdom, then likewise one should meditate on the absorption of the arising of Vajraratna in its proper sequence. Meditating at the four times on this absorption called "Just as a jewel arises from insubstantial space, so the body of the bodhisattva arises from the jewel," one should recite as stated above. Then, at the end of the month, one should recite throughout one whole night with that very absorption. Then, when dawn breaks, all the tathāgatas will come and confer empowerment as one wishes. Thus spoke the Blessed One Ākāśagarbha.

Then the accomplishment of action is explained. One recites in the same way, and at the end of the month one should recite throughout one whole night. Then, when dawn breaks, through the Blessed One Ākāśagarbha all aims will be accomplished. Then one will perform all actions. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

The extensive ritual of ancillary practice for the jewel family is complete.

Then, this is the extensive account of the requisites and the rite of accomplishment common to all the families. There, first, the requisites and rite of all the heart mantras are explained in detail. If one wishes to accomplish a heart mantra, one should say, "May it be accomplished by all the tathāgatas!" and then recite the heart mantra as one likes and perform the rite of accomplishment.

There, these are the requisites and rite of the mudrās. One should say, "May all the mudrās be pleased with me!" and, binding the samaya mudrā, should recite as one likes, as much as one is able. Then accomplishment will come about just as it should.

There, this is the extensive account of the requisites and rite of all mantras. One says, "May speech free of conceptual elaboration be accomplished! May the Absorption of All Tathāgatas arise in me!" and recites the mantra as one likes. This is the accomplishment.

There, this is the extensive account of the requisites and rite of the vidyā. One says, "O, all these sentient beings are blinded by ignorance, while all the tathāgatas have realized knowledge and are restrained by the vow," and one should recite and accomplish the vidyā as one likes. Thus spoke the Blessed One, the noble Vajradhara.

Then, this is the extensive rite of vajra recitation, by which all heart mantras, mantras, mudrās, and vidyās are recited just as they are meditated upon. Whatever is to be accomplished, with whatever intention, by reciting heart mantras, mudrās, secret mantras, and vidyās, if one meditates on oneself or on the sattva, what is to be accomplished will be accomplished. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

The extensive rite of recitation common to all the families is complete.

Then, the extensive rite of accomplishment common to all the families is as follows. There, first of all, the accomplishments of the family of the tathāgatas are these: the accomplishment of gaining wealth, the accomplishment of magical power, the attainment of the state of a vidyādhara, and the great accomplishments.

There, the gaining of wealth is this. Wherever one suspects there is a treasure, one binds the mudrā and, by means of one's own absorption, looks at that treasure site with the vajra gaze. If one sees something like a vajra gleaming there, one should know that there is a treasure in that place. Then, binding the samaya mudrā of Vajrasphoṭa, one digs the earth and takes it as one pleases, and one will obtain it without dispute. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

There, this is the attainment of the accomplishment of magical power. Whatever mudrā one is accomplishing, one should say "vajra siddhi." The accomplishment of magical power is of four kinds, namely these: going to and fro and remaining upon water and the like; displaying the forms of all, the tathāgatas and the rest; becoming invisible for as long as one wishes; going through the sky; soaring a thousand leagues (yojanas) above; going and coming beneath the earth; going a thousand leagues in every direction as one likes, circling about, and coming back again; knowing the thoughts of all sentient beings for a thousand leagues all around; seeing all things with the eye and hearing them with the ear; taking upon one's lap, as one pleases, all the women dwelling within a thousand leagues around in every direction; and even after carrying off all riches, such as gold, gems, and pearls, and all wealth, no one at all is able to challenge one in the least. Since not even my vajra could prevail, what need is there to speak of others? One will have the strength of ten thousand men combined, will always be free of illness, will always enjoy all objects of desire, will always be in the prime of youth and have the form of a god, will see all the tathāgatas together with Vajrasattva, will make offerings with the unsurpassed vajra accomplishment, and will live for fourteen thousand years. Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Vajrasiddhi of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the attainment of the accomplishment of the vajra vidyādhara. When accomplishing the mudrā, one says "Vajra Vidyādhara," and it is accomplished, and one becomes a wheel-turning sovereign of the vajra vidyādharas. One will enjoy all objects of desire, will circle through all the buddha fields in an instant and return, and will fully enjoy every bliss. One will have the youth of an eighteen-year-old and will wear coiled locks. Having become a great vajra vidyādhara, one will see all the tathāgatas and all the vajrasattvas and will live for a great eon. Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Vidyādhara of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the attainment of the great accomplishment. If, for the sake of the heart of one's own mudrā, one accomplishes the mudrā by oneself, one becomes the form of the sattva, and even in a single instant one performs various deeds with various forms in all the world systems of the ten directions. Seeing all the tathāgatas together with Vajrasattva, one worships them perfectly with the offerings of the tathāgatas, endowed with all the supreme aspects. Having worked for the benefit of all sentient beings, without exception and without remainder, one comes back again and, throughout all the world systems, enjoys all the objects of desire, all pleasure, and all mental happiness, endowed with all the supreme aspects. Having become a bodhisattva, a great being equal to Vajrasattva, one's lifespan will reach the measure of great eons, without exception and without remainder. Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Great Accomplishment of the Tathāgatas' Means.

There, these are the accomplishments of the vajra family, namely: the accomplishment of victory over the three worlds, the accomplishment of all empowerments, the accomplishment of all pleasure and mental happiness, and the supreme accomplishments.

There, this is the accomplishment of victory over the three worlds. Binding the mudrā of victory over the three realms, one should accomplish it while pressing down Maheśvara (Śiva) with the left foot. Then, when that image emits a sound, one should say "hūṃ." By merely saying "hūṃ," the lords of all the three worlds, Maheśvara and the others, together with their retinues, will obey before the practitioner and come under control. From then on one becomes Vajradhara, the lord of all the three worlds. Going through the sky, one circles through all the spheres of the three worlds and, coming back again, tames with "hūṃ" all sentient beings, such as malevolent gods. Having the form called Vajra Hūṃ of All Tathāgatas, one issues commands to all the three worlds, sees all the tathāgatas together with Vajrasattva and pleases them, and lives for a thousand years. Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrahūṃkāra.

There, this is the accomplishment of all empowerments. When accomplishing the mudrā, one should first empower oneself with the samaya mudrā of the jewel of the empowerment of all the tathāgatas, and then accomplish it. Then, once it is accomplished, the empowerment becomes fourfold: the vajra empowerment, the jewel empowerment, the Dharma empowerment, and the karma empowerment. There, if one obtains the vajra empowerment, one becomes the vajra holder of all the tathāgatas. If one obtains the jewel empowerment, one becomes the lord of all jewels. Through the Dharma empowerment one becomes the king of all dharmas. Through the karma empowerment one attains the accomplishment of all supramundane actions. Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Empowerment of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the accomplishment of all pleasure and mental happiness. It is as follows: if one always makes offerings to all the tathāgatas with the secret offerings, one will attain the accomplishment of all the pleasure and mental happiness of all the tathāgatas. Thus spoke the Blessed One, the All Pleasure and Mental Happiness of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the supreme accomplishment. It is this: "I am equal to Vajradhara." Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

There, these are the accomplishments of the lotus family, namely: pleasing, bringing under control, protection, and the lotus accomplishments.

There, the accomplishment of pleasing is this. If one meditates on Padma Kāma, one will be able to please all sentient beings, beginning with all the tathāgatas. If one recollects oneself as Lokeśvara, one will be able to bring all sentient beings under control. Through the absorption of pervading with loving-kindness, one will be able to protect, conceal, and shelter all beings. If, oneself entering the lotus absorption, one takes a lotus in the hand and accomplishes it, one will have the form of Lokeśvara, be endowed with all the supreme aspects, and live for forty thousand years. Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Lotus of All Tathāgatas.

There, these are the accomplishments of the jewel family, namely: the accomplishment of the empowerment of all the families, great splendor, the complete fulfillment of all wishes, and the jewel accomplishments. The accomplishment of the empowerment of all the tathāgatas is as follows. If one makes offerings to all the tathāgatas by offering empowerment oneself, by offering lamps, by perfecting the perfection of generosity as far as one is able, and by these accomplishments of accomplishing the jewel, it will be accomplished before long. Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Jewel of All Tathāgatas.

The extensive rite of the accomplishments common to all the families is complete.

Then, "I shall explain the tantra of the accomplishment of the means of all the rites," thus spoke the Blessed One, the Sattva Without Beginning or End.

There, first of all is the tantra of the accomplishment of all the heart mantras:

Just as the world is trained,
So too is accomplishment held to be.
Its means is the mudrā;
It bestows all the great accomplishments.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the means of mudrā:

The training of the world is not free of passion;
The accomplishment of mudrā is born from passion.
Its means is meditation;
It becomes the highest of all accomplishments.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the means of mantra:

Because this world has fallen away from truth,
It does not attain the accomplishment of mantra.
But through the means free of conceptual elaboration,
It becomes the highest of all accomplishments.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of all the vidyās:

For this world, sunk in the darkness of ignorance,
There is no accomplishment of the vidyā;
But through its means, which is vast,
One attains the highest of all accomplishments.

This was the tantra of the accomplishment of all the rites.

Then, "I shall explain the tantra of the accomplishment of merit of all the rites," thus spoke all the blessed tathāgatas.

There, first of all is the tantra of the accomplishment of merit of all the heart mantras:

Having made the fourfold offering,
One will gain great merit.
Even without merit, worship the buddhas;
One will succeed: of this there is no doubt.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of merit of mudrā:

If one guards the secret samaya,
One will gain great merit.
Even without merit, swiftly,
If one guards the samaya, one swiftly succeeds.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of merit of mantra:

If one recites the names of the buddhas and so on,
One will gain great merit.
Even without merit, swiftly,
Through the giving of the Dharma one attains accomplishment.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of merit of the vidyā:

Among the kinds of merit, giving is supreme;
If one gives, one will gain merit.
If the perfection of generosity is completed,
One swiftly accomplishes buddhahood itself.

This was the tantra of the accomplishment of merit of all the rites.

Then, "I shall explain the tantra of the accomplishment of wisdom of all the rites," thus spoke all the blessed tathāgatas.

There, first of all is the tantra of the accomplishment of wisdom of the heart mantras:

Entering into the letter A,
Meditating on all the letters,
From one's own mouth into the mouth of the other,
If one meditates thus, one will gain merit.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the great bodhisattva Mañjuśrī.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of wisdom of mudrā:

It is renowned as the absorption
Called the Penetration of Wisdom.
If one meditates on it so the mudrā is swiftly accomplished,
It will be swiftly accomplished.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Supreme Wisdom.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of wisdom of mantra:

Wisdom follows after sound;
The words of absorption are fully elaborated.
If one meditates on that, swiftly indeed
One will attain the accomplishment of mantra.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajramati.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of wisdom of the vidyā:

Between the vidyā and the mantra
There is no particular difference.
If one meditates on wisdom, swiftly
One will surely attain accomplishment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Wisdom Gnosis of All Tathāgatas.

This was the tantra of the extensive rite of the accomplishment of gnosis of all the rites.

Then, "I shall explain the tantra of the accomplishment of the accumulations of all the rites," thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi.

There, first of all is the tantra of the accomplishment of the accumulations of all the heart mantras:

If one makes all the offerings well,
The accumulation of virtuous dharmas
Will greatly increase,
And then the accumulation will be accomplished.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the accumulations of mudrā:

Because one enters many circles
And draws many maṇḍalas,
The accumulations are completed, and so
One will attain the great accomplishment of the mudrās.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the accumulations of mantra:

Through the ways of rejoicing and the rest,
Likewise by reading the sublime Dharma,
And by doing much recitation,
One will surely attain the accomplishment of mantra.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the accumulations of the vidyā:

Through fully abandoning ignorance
And through the way of the perfection of generosity,
The accumulations being fully completed,
One will swiftly attain accomplishment.

The tantra of the accomplishment of the accumulations of all the rites is complete.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, spoke this tantra of the accomplishment of the gnosis from which the signs of all the families arise:

There, if one asks how the vajra arises:
That very one is the Blessed One, the sattva,
The lord who is the very mind of all,
The vajra of body, speech, and mind.
The sattva is self-arisen and steadfast,
The sattva supreme among all sattvas.
If one meditates well on the vajra at the heart,
It is renowned as Vajrasattva;
In this the vajra firmly abides.
That itself is the way of the gnosis
Of the buddhas, without equal.
Arising in variety from the heart,
It becomes forms of every variety;
As many as the atoms of all the realms,
These very ones are the victors.
From them the vajra body
Truly becomes Vajrasattva.
From that very first sattva
All the signs truly arise.
From the signs comes the great sattva;
From him arises this whole way.
Whoever hears this,
Has faith, and holds it, and at the heart
Always meditates on it with joy,
They will swiftly attain accomplishment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

This was the tantra of the extensive rite of the gnosis from which arises the reality of all the tathāgatas.

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi spoke this tantra of the extensive rite of the gnosis from which all the rites arise.

There, first of all, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which all the rites of all the tathāgatas arise:

The gnosis of the buddhas
Always arises from the conception of ripening.
Because it comes from nonconceptual gnosis,
It is therefore called permanent.

There, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which the rite of the tathāgata family arises:

Where the nonconceptual is conceived
As conception itself, what arises from conception
Is Vajrasattva, the great sattva;
Therefore it is called a rite (kalpa).

There, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which the rite of the vajra family arises:

Drawn as an image according to the ritual,
And conceived as "the rite that grants accomplishment,"
It is so through a nature free of conception;
Therefore it is called a rite.

There, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which the rite of the lotus family arises:

From conception, passion arises;
In the lotus it abides.
Therefore what abides in conception
Becomes accomplishment itself.

There, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which the rite of the jewel family arises:

The jewel that is free of conception
Becomes most supreme in power.
Thus, through nonconceptuality,
Divine substances and accomplishment will arise.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

This was the tantra of the arising of the rites of all the families.

Then, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which the heart mantras of all the rites arise:

Through the rite of what the mind desires,
What the mind desires is accomplished.
The accomplishment of absorption abides in the heart;
Therefore it is called the heart.

Then, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which the mudrās of all the rites arise:

Just as the supreme seal of a king
Is a law that cannot be transgressed or broken,
So by the image of the great being
The sign is renowned as the mudrā, the seal.

Then, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which the mantras of all the rites arise:

It is not to be transgressed,
Hard to break open, and secret in itself;
Because it is uttered to accomplish what is secret,
For that reason it is called mantra.

Then, this is the tantra of the gnosis from which the vidyā arises:

In order to destroy ignorance
And to accomplish the supreme knowledge of speech,
Realizing and knowing are the accomplishment;
Therefore it is called a rite.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajradhara.

This was the tantra of the gnosis from which the heart mantras and the rest of all the rites arise.

Then, this is the tantra of the gnosis of all the rites. There, first of all, is the tantra of the arising of the gnosis of the heart mantra:

When, having recited the heart mantra,
It arises in oneself or likewise in another,
Know that whatever has happened and will happen
Will be seen and will be heard.

There, this is the tantra of the arising of the gnosis of mudrā:

Following my rite exactly,
Bind any mudrā whatever;
Having looked at a person of the world,
One will know everything, as above.

There, this is the tantra of the arising of the gnosis of mantra:

If one utters the mantra even once,
Whatever is past, is arising, or has come about,
That is spoken upon one's own tongue,
All of it will prove true.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Avalokiteśvara, who looks on all.

There, this is the tantra of the arising of the gnosis of the vidyā:

If one utters the vidyā once,
What has arisen, is arising, and will arise,
Just as by the command of the vajra,
One will know it with the mind.

The tantra of the extensive rite of the arising of the gnosis of all the rites is complete.

Then, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret vajra mudrās of body, speech, and mind common to all the families.

There, this is the accomplishment of the body mudrā of the tathāgata family:

Seated in whatever way one sits,
One should practice in the cross-legged posture.
Having done so in the manner of the painted image,
One will accomplish the great sattva.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

There, this is the accomplishment of the secret body mudrā of the vajra family:

Just as in the manner of the painted image,
One stands firm in the manner of the eightfold practice.
If one practices in fierce wrath,
One will succeed without doubt.

Thus spoke the Blessed One called Vajra Hūṃ.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret body mudrā of the lotus family:

Seated in the vajra cross-legged posture,
Binding the two hands in the vajra,
Becoming the body of absorption,
One should practice the arising of the lotus.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Padmasattva.

There, this is the accomplishment of the secret body mudrā of the jewel family:

Whether sitting or lying down,
Or walking, whichever it may be,
If one is empowered by Vajraratna,
One will succeed without doubt.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajragarbha.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret speech mudrā of the family of all the tathāgatas:

Keeping the tip of the tongue quite unmoving,
And joining the teeth and the two lips,
One should practice all the rites;
This is the soundless vajra speech.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajravāk.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret speech mudrā of the vajra family:

Proclaiming "hūṃ" like thunder,
The speech of wrath, profound and displayed,
Clear with the wrath of the great vajra:
The speech of Vajrakrodha is accomplished.

Thus spoke the Blessed One called Vajra Hūṃ.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret speech mudrā of the lotus family:

Breathing out as though not breathing out,
With subtle words that are very clear,
Through the heart of the gnosis of absorption,
Those who recite will be accomplished.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Heart of the Gnosis of the Absorption of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret speech mudrā of the jewel family:

With words that are utterly clear,
Always striving in homage,
If one recites in this manner,
Everything will swiftly be accomplished.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Vajra of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret mind mudrā of the tathāgata family:

The Blessed One, ever desire itself,
Bestows bliss on all sentient beings;
If one meditates on oneself as Vajrasattva,
One will swiftly succeed.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Kāma.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret mind mudrā of the vajra family:

To benefit all sentient beings,
To tame the malevolent,
And to protect the teaching of the Buddha,
One will attain the wrathful accomplishment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One called Vajra Hūṃ of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret mind mudrā of the lotus family:

Just as a lotus, fully grown,
Is not stained by the faults of what it is steeped in,
So, even if I have the fault of passion,
That passion will become accomplishment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Blossoming Lotus.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret mind mudrā of the jewel family:

"When shall I accomplish every needed aim
Of all sentient beings,
And rain down a rain of jewels?"
When this is accomplished, it is swiftly achieved.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Ākāśagarbha.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret vajra mudrā of the tathāgata family:

Whether standing or sitting,
Or walking, whichever it may be,
If with the left hand one performs
All the supreme mudrās, one will succeed.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Secret Mudrā Vajra of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret vajra mudrā of the vajra family:

Remaining however one may remain,
And doing whatever one may do,
Binding the finger mudrā of Vajrakrodha,
If one covers it with cloth, one will succeed.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the King of Wrath of All Tathāgatas.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret vajra mudrā of the lotus family:

Binding the lotus fist
With the left hand and concealing it,
As one wishes, swiftly,
One will attain the lotus accomplishment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Avalokiteśvara, who looks on all.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret vajra mudrā of the jewel family:

Binding the jewel fist of the left hand,
And covering it with a fine hand-covering,
One performs service as one pleases;
One will attain the jewel accomplishment.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Ākāśagarbha.

There, this is the tantra of the accomplishment of the secret mudrā common to all the families:

Through the absorption of one's own sattva,
In the manner of the Great Mudrā,
The vajras and the vajra gaze:
Before long the supreme is accomplished.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, the Vajrasattva of All Tathāgatas.

The tantra of the accomplishment of the secret vajra mudrās of body, speech, and mind of all the families is complete.

Then the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi called out to all the tathāgatas and said, "Blessed tathāgatas, all of you, please consider blessing this rite."

Then all the tathāgatas gathered together once more and gave this "Excellent!":

Because you have well explained this sūtra,
The unsurpassed vajra vehicle,
The secret of all the tathāgatas,
The Realization of the Great Vehicle,
Excellent, Vajrasattva, are you!
Vajraratna, you are excellent!
Vajradharma, you are excellent,
And Vajrakarma, you are excellent!

From the Compendium of the Reality of All Tathāgatas, the further subsequent tantra of all the tantras is complete.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, rose from his seat and supplicated the Blessed One with these hundred and eight names:

Great sattva of the vajra realm,
Whose nature is the supreme benefit of beings,
King of the Śākyas, great in gnosis,
You whose nature is vajra, homage to you.

Source of sattvas, the very meaning of reality,
Cause of sentient beings, the great way,
Great benefit of beings, the aim that is needed,
Sattva of sattvas, homage to you.

Dharma of the buddhas, the great Dharma,
Who fully turns the wheel of Dharma,
Great speaker, supreme in knowing,
Great sattva, homage to you.

Great action, great protection,
Who fully accomplishes all actions,
Great being, supreme in conduct among beings,
Cause of sattvas, homage to you.

You who have attained all the perfections,
Knower of the gnosis of omniscience,
All the sattvas, the great means,
Great wisdom, homage to you.

Great compassion, supreme of the supreme,
Whose nature is the compassion of compassion,
Giver of all, great in loving-kindness,
Maker of all, homage to you.

Lion of the Śākyas, great Śākya,
Śākya, great Śākyamuni,
Great tamer, all-pervading,
Supreme tamer for the sake of beings, homage to you.

You who have attained equality with the dharmadhātu,
The dharmadhātu, the Tathāgata,
Vajra protector, great protector,
Host of sentient beings, homage to you.

Great light, great illumination,
Great diligence, great power,
Great hero, supreme in heroism,
Hero of blissful conduct, homage to you.

Brahmā, self-arisen, Blessed One,
Hero of the Śākyas, great sage,
Lord of all, of the pure sages
The Dharma king, homage to you.

Body of space, supreme of bodies,
Meditator on the three bodies and on no body,
Body of all, great body,
Vajra body, homage to you.

Speech that is no speech, supreme of speech,
Teacher of the three speeches and of no speech,
All speech, exceedingly great speech,
Vajra speech, homage to you.

Mind that is no mind, supreme of minds,
Teacher of the three minds and of no mind,
Mind of all, great mind,
Vajra mind, homage to you.

Vajra that is no vajra, supreme of vajras,
Triple vajra, purifier of what is not vajra,
All vajras, great vajra,
Vajra of vajras, homage to you.

All-pervading, supreme summit of existence,
Lord of the sugatas, the victor,
Great king of the three realms,
Vairocana, homage to you.

These hundred and eight names:
Whoever listens to them for an instant,
Or reads them, or else meditates on them,
All of them will attain buddhahood itself.

Protector, I entreat you:
For the benefit of all sentient beings,
Generating great compassion,
Fully turn the wheel of Dharma!

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, having heard the words spoken by the lord of all the tathāgatas, called out to all the tathāgatas and said, "Blessed tathāgatas, please gather together once more."

Then all the tathāgatas gathered together once more and spoke this verse:

Always, in all the worlds,
For the benefit of all sentient beings,
Fully turn the wheel of Dharma,
Varied according to those to be trained!

Then, immediately after this was spoken, the Blessed Tathāgata Śākyamuni, having pervaded everywhere with maṇḍalas as numerous as all the minute atoms, before all the sentient beings of all the buddha fields and all the world systems, fully turned the wheel of Dharma.

Then again the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi spoke this verse:

Always, in all the worlds,
For the benefit of all sentient beings,
Fully turn the vajra wheel,
Varied according to those to be trained!

Then, immediately after this was spoken, likewise, in maṇḍalas as numerous as all the minute atoms of all the buddha fields, the Blessed Tathāgata Vajradhātu fully turned all the vajra wheels, beginning with the Vajradhātu.

Then, at that time, the great bodhisattva Trailokyavijaya spoke this verse:

Always, in all the worlds,
For the benefit of all sentient beings,
Fully turn the wrathful wheel,
Varied according to those to be trained!

Then, immediately after this was spoken, likewise, in maṇḍalas as numerous as all the minute atoms of the buddha fields, the Blessed Tathāgata Trailokyavijaya fully turned the wrathful wheel of all the tathāgatas.

Then the great bodhisattva, the noble Samantāvalokiteśvara, spoke this verse:

Always, in all the worlds,
For the benefit of all sentient beings,
Fully turn the lotus wheel,
Varied according to those to be trained!

Then, immediately after this was spoken, likewise, in maṇḍalas as numerous as the most minute atoms of all the buddha fields, the Blessed Tathāgata, the King of Dharma, fully turned the lotus wheel.

Then the great bodhisattva, the noble Ākāśagarbha, spoke this verse:

Always, in all the worlds,
For the benefit of all sentient beings,
Fully turn the jewel wheel,
Varied according to those to be trained!

Then, at that time, all the sentient beings included within all the buddha fields, whether subtle or gross, all of them saw, from every direction, all the tathāgatas seated on their seats in the many-storied pavilion made of vajra, jewel and precious substances on the summit of Sumeru, king of mountains, fully turning all the wheels, beginning with the Vajradhātu.

Then, at that time, the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi spoke this verse:

Having descended among humans as well,
Because it is needed, for the benefit of all beings,
Turn the supreme vajra wheel,
And, O Protector, bring it to full accomplishment!

Then again the Blessed One summoned the tathāgatas and spoke these words: "Blessed tathāgatas, may all of you please gather into one once more."

Then again all the blessed tathāgatas gathered into one and entered the heart of the Blessed Vairocana.

Then the Blessed Vairocana, who is all the tathāgatas, knowing that he had become the very nature of the vajra of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, spoke these words to Vajrapāṇi: "Son of noble family, you too enter my heart, and all the tathāgatas, all the families, all the maṇḍalas, and the heart of all the tathāgatas."

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, by the radiance of all the tathāgatas, spoke this verse:

In brief, the vajra of the body,
Speech and mind of all the buddhas,
Having been entered, should be accomplished
For the benefit of all sentient beings.

Then, immediately after this was spoken, in all the world systems equal in number to the most minute atoms of all the world systems, of all the tathāgatas dwelling there, each tathāgata emanated bodies equal in number to the most minute atoms of all the world systems, and these too entered the heart of the Blessed Vairocana.

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi entered and abided in all the tathāgatas, and in all the bodies of the Blessed Vairocana, in the place where all speech enters, in every arising of the mind stream, in all the vajra modes, in the limbs and all the minor limbs, in all the places, in all the major marks, in all the excellent minor marks, in all the pores of the skin, in all the maṇḍalas as numerous as the most minute atoms, and in the hearts.

Then, not long after the Blessed One had become perfectly and completely awakened, knowing that in all the tathāgatas he had himself become the vajra of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, he went from the summit of Mount Sumeru to the seat of awakening. There, at the foot of the Blessed One's tree of awakening, in order to conform to the ways of the world, he took up grass and uttered this solemn utterance:

How wonderful! For a steadfast being,
The benefit of beings is the highest benefit of self.
Because the steadfast, as those to be trained require,
Abide even in the views of non-Buddhists (tīrthikas).
In brief, it is to purify with the light of wisdom
Worldly beings who are untamed
And blinded by wrong views
That buddhas attain awakening.

Then the gods who move in the desire realm, not knowing reality, said this to the Blessed One: "O ascetic, is it for the sake of awakening that you endure such fierce and unbearable suffering?"

Then the Blessed One spread out that grass, sat upon it, and declared to the gods: "Friends, bring about my attainment of awakening!"

Then the sons of the gods who move in the desire realm, not understanding the meaning of what the Blessed One had said, went to where Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, was, and told Śakra, lord of the gods, what had happened.

Then Śakra, lord of the gods, surrounded by the hosts of gods who move in the desire realm, told these happenings to Great Brahmā, lord of the gods who move in the form realm.

Then Great Brahmā, together with all the gods who move in the desire realm and in the form realm, told these happenings to Maheśvara (Śiva), lord of all three worlds.

Then Maheśvara, lord of the three worlds in their entirety, spoke these words to the lords of the gods, the Mighty One and the others: "Friends, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete buddha, conforms to the world, and moreover teaches unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. Do not regard him thus: 'He is not a tathāgata in the world.' It is from being a god that one becomes a tathāgata, so he is to be accomplished as one of those very gods, and not as a tathāgata. That being well, let us set out to go there to make offerings."

Then Maheśvara, lord of all the gods, and the others went to the seat of awakening, where the Blessed Tathāgata was dwelling at the heart of awakening. There they bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One and said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, out of loving concern for us, please rise from this seat of grass, sit upon a seat of divine substance, and become fully and manifestly awakened in unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening."

Then the Blessed One declared to those lords of the gods: "Friends, bring about the bestowal of unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening upon me!"

Then they said this: "Blessed One, we are unable to bestow it. If we were able, we ourselves would become perfectly and completely awakened buddhas."

Then the Blessed One, making that very point clear, again spoke this verse:

It is not form, nor is it not form;
It is not true, nor is it false.
This pure wisdom of the buddhas' awakening:
If one realizes it, one becomes a Victor.

Then those lords of the gods remained for a little while without saying anything at all.

Then the Blessed One rose from the seat of grass and declared to those gods: "Friends, bring about the realization of such buddha wisdom!"

They said: "Blessed One, we are not capable of it."

Then again the Blessed One, remaining on that very seat, spoke this verse:

Having fully understood with the mind,
Make firm the mind of awakening!
Having made firm the being-vajra,
Meditate on yourselves as buddhas!

Then they all said, "Such is excellent!" and departed.

Then, at dawn of that night, in order to conform to the ways of the world, the Blessed One defeated Māra together with his host and became manifestly awakened as an unsurpassed, perfect and complete buddha. Then, benefiting the entire realm of sentient beings, without exception and without remainder, he praised the noble Vajrapāṇi, who had entered his own heart, with these hundred and eight names:

Vajrasattva, great being,
Great Vehicle, great-souled one,
Great in light and great in purity,
Great Protector, homage to you!

Vajrarāja, great vajra,
Vajra who are all the tathāgatas,
Great being, great in diligence,
Great Means, homage to you!

Vajrakāma, great in purity,
Bliss of all, great bliss,
Supreme bliss without beginning or end,
Great Laughter, homage to you!

Vajrasādhu, great in delight,
Supremely pleased, saying "Excellent!",
Great in pleasure and great in delight,
Supremely Delighted One, homage to you!

Vajraratna, great king,
Excellent empowerment, great lord,
Great in beauty with all the jewels,
Victorious One, homage to you!

Vajrateja, great in splendor,
Great light, great brilliance,
Light of the Victors, greatly blazing,
Light of the Buddha, homage to you!

Vajraketu, great in glory,
With the great banner bestowing wealth abundantly,
Banner of space with its great shaft,
Banner of Giving, homage to you!

Vajrahāsa, great laughter,
Great in joy and supremely joyful,
Playing and rejoicing through the force of joy,
Joy in the Dharma, homage to you!

Vajradharma, great Dharma,
Who thoroughly purifies the nature of beings,
Dharma of the buddhas, supreme Dharma,
Dharma of Desire, homage to you!

Vajratīkṣṇa, great sword,
Great in discernment, wisdom and intellect,
Great sword that cuts away evil,
Weapon of the Buddha, homage to you!

Vajrahetu, great wheel,
Wheel of the buddhas, great treasure,
All the maṇḍalas, supreme Dharma,
Maṇḍala of the Dharma, homage to you!

Vajravāk, great in speech,
Great syllable free of elaboration,
Great recitation without syllables,
Speech of the Buddha, homage to you!

Vajrakarma, supreme action,
Great action, thoroughly performing action,
Secret worship, great worship,
Worship of the Buddha, homage to you!

Vajrarakṣa, great armor,
Supreme armor, great steadfastness,
Great protection and great essence,
Diligence of the Buddha, homage to you!

Vajrayakṣa, great wrathful one,
Who terrifies all the wicked,
Great means of all the buddhas,
Supreme Protection, homage to you!

Vajra Bond, great seal,
Great pledge, great binding,
Great fist, great seal,
Vajramuṣṭi, homage to you!

The Tathāgata should be bowed to,
Proclaimed, worshipped and honored,
Because, without beginning or end,
You are the mind of awakening.
Relying on you, the Victors
And all the heroes, the bodhisattvas,
Have arisen and will truly arise;
Therefore, cause of the supreme awakening of the buddhas,
Vajrasattva, homage to you!
Vajraratna, homage to you!
Vajradharma, homage to you!
Vajrakarma, homage to you!

Whatever merit there is
In rightly bowing to you
And praising you with these supreme names,
Through all of it may the awakening of the buddhas be attained!

Whoever, with full faith,
Even just once,
Properly recites these sacred hundred and eight names,
All of them will become buddhas.

Thus spoke the Buddha, the Blessed One.

Then all the tathāgatas, gathered into one, gave to the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, with one voice, this acclamation of "Excellent!":

The unsurpassed Vajra Vehicle,
The secret of all the tathāgatas:
Because you have well explained
This sūtra, the Realization of the Great Vehicle,
Excellent are you, Vajrasattva!
Excellent are you, Vajraratna!
Excellent are you, Vajradharma!
Excellent are you, Vajrakarma!

When the Blessed One had spoken thus, all the tathāgatas, together with the noble bodhisattvas, who had entered his heart, were delighted and pleased, and rejoiced greatly at what the Blessed Vajrasattva had spoken.

This completes the Great Vehicle sūtra called the Compendium of the Reality of All Tathāgatas, together with the abridged tantra.

Although no translators' colophon appears for this tantra, it is known to have been translated by the paṇḍita Śraddhākaravarman and the Tibetan translator Rinchen Zangpo.

Translator's notes

The verses are a terse list of names and emblems; the sixteen items "vajra hook" through "binding" correspond to the emblems of the sixteen vajra bodhisattvas, and many of the preceding names are epithets of those bodhisattvas. "Śiva" renders གུ་ལང, an epithet of Śiva. གཏོགས་འདོད and རྒྱལ་འདོད are translated by meaning ("Desiring Inclusion," "Desiring Victory") because their Sanskrit equivalents could not be identified with confidence. "Avalokiteśvara the all-seeing lord" renders ཀུན་ཏུ་སྤྱན་རས་གཟིགས་ཀྱི་དབང་པོ, which adds "all" to the usual form of the name. The opening verses continue the list of names and epithets begun in the previous piece. The lines are terse, and "the supreme ground," "the great trunk" and "lord of abodes" are rendered literally. "The Great Black One" renders ནག་པོ་ཆེ (Mahākāla) as a descriptive epithet in this list. The bodhisattva named གདོན་མི་ཟ་བའི་རྒྱལ་པོ is rendered Amogharāja ("Unfailing King"), the bodhisattva form of Vajrarāja; the one mention without རྒྱལ་པོ ("became the body of the great bodhisattva Amogha") follows the Tibetan as it stands. The bodhisattva called Māra here is the form of Vajrarāga, the deity of passion, not the adversary Māra; "supremely the work of Māra" renders ཤིན་ཏུ་བདུད་བྱེད་པ, playing on the name. In "became the great bodhisattva Māra" the text reads བདུད་བྱ་བར, evidently for བདུད་ཅེས་བྱ་བར "called Māra". The bodhisattva རབ་ཏུ་དགའ་བའི་རྒྱལ་པོ ("King of Utter Joy") is given as Prāmodyarāja, and the vajra name རྡོ་རྗེ་དགྱེས་པ as Vajraharṣa (Vajra Delight), as in the Sanskrit of this tantra; it is not the deity Hevajra. "In his two hands" reads ཕྱག for the written བྱག. The last line of Vajraharṣa's verse, ལྷ་རྫས་དགྱེས་པ་སྤེལ་བའོ, is somewhat obscure (literally "increases delight, divine substance") and is rendered by its evident sense. The bodhisattva གཟི་བརྗིད་ཆེན་པོ is Mahātejas. His vajra name, written རྡོ་རྗེའི་འོད ("Vajra Light"), is given as Vajratejas, matching his mantra and the parallel names in this series. རིན་པོ་ཆེ་དཔལ / རིན་པོ་ཆེའི་དཔལ ("Jewel Glory") is given as Ratnaśrī, following the Tibetan. This bodhisattva corresponds to the one known elsewhere as Ratnaketu, whose seed mantra here is vajraketu. "The empowerment of the sprout of the vajra jewel" renders མྱུ་གུ literally. The bodhisattva རྟག་པར་དགའ་ཞིང་རབ་ཏུ་མགུ་བའི་དབང་པོ ("Ever-Joyful One of Delighted Faculties") is given as Nityaprītipramuditendriya. His vajra name, written རྡོ་རྗེ་དགའ་བ ("Vajra Joy"), is given as Vajrahāsa, matching his mantra and his emblem, the vajra smile. "Happiness of mind" reads ཡིད་བདེ་བ for the written ལིང་བདེ་བ. Mañjuśrī's verse plays on his names: the samādhi is "supremely gentle glory" (the meaning of Mañjuśrī), he calls himself the Gentle Voice (Mañjughoṣa), and the verse explains that formless discernment is perceived as voice. The bodhisattva's name Sahacittotpādadharmacakrapravartin means "he who turns the wheel of Dharma as soon as the mind is raised." The bodhisattva called here ཚིག་མ་ཡིན་པ ("not words") is the same figure named earlier as མི་གསུང་བ (Sanskrit Avāca), so both are rendered "the Speechless One." The vajra name རྡོ་རྗེ་ཚིག ("vajra word") is given as Vajrabhāṣa, the name this bodhisattva receives in the Sanskrit Sarvatathāgatatattvasaṃgraha, matching his heart mantra. The "vajra of all actions" is the crossed (karma) vajra. "Seat of awakening" renders བྱང་ཆུབ་སྙིང་པོ (bodhimaṇḍa). གདོན་མི་ཟ་བ is the idiom "unfailing, certain" (Skt. amogha), not a reference to seizing spirits, and is rendered accordingly. The bodhisattva's name "All the Activities of All the Tathāgatas" is translated literally from the Tibetan, as its exact Sanskrit form is not certain from this text. Duryodhanavīrya ("hard to overcome in diligence"), Vajraviśva, Vajramitra and Sarvamārapramardin ("crusher of all māras") are the Sanskrit names known for these figures in the Sarvatathāgatatattvasaṃgraha. The vajra name རྡོ་རྗེ་ཁྲོ་བོ ("vajra wrathful one") is given as Vajracaṇḍa, the name this bodhisattva bears in the Sanskrit Sarvatathāgatatattvasaṃgraha. Sarvatathāgatamuṣṭi ("fist of all the tathāgatas") likewise follows the Sanskrit tradition of this tantra. The name of the goddess, རྡོ་རྗེ་འཛིན་མ ("she who holds the vajra"), is translated by meaning, since its Sanskrit form is not certain from this text. The four mudrā deities that emerge here are the vajra perfections (sattvavajrī, ratnavajrī, dharmavajrī, karmavajrī) that surround Vairocana in the Vajradhātu maṇḍala. In the Vajradhūpā verse, དབབ ("descent") is taken as the descent or entry of the deity into the practitioner (Skt. āveśa); the line is terse. The goddesses' names (Vajramālā, Vajragītā, Vajranṛtyā, Vajradhūpā, Vajrapuṣpā) are the standard Sanskrit names of these offering goddesses of the Vajradhātu maṇḍala. Ratnasaṃbhava and Lokeśvararāja appear here in slightly variant Tibetan spellings and are kept the same. The last two lines of Vajrapuṣpā's verse are terse; they may also be read "for by offering, the tathāgatas are swiftly accomplished as jewels." The rendering given takes the offering as the means by which the offerer swiftly attains the jewel-nature of the tathāgatas. The five scents listed (moral discipline, samādhi, wisdom, liberation, and the wisdom and vision of liberation) are the traditional five uncontaminated aggregates of the dharmakāya. The gatekeeper called "vajra chain" in Tibetan is given his name in this text, Vajrasphoṭa, matching the mantra spoken for him in the preceding passage. In the description of Vajrāveśa's seat, the text's "gate" (sgo gang) is read as "gatehouse" (sgo khang), as in the parallel phrases. The phrase "set out and went" renders a terse expression (literally "having stayed, went with ease") whose exact sense is uncertain; the gist, that the assembled tathāgatas came to Vairocana's pavilion, is clear. In the maṇḍala verses the Tibetan mi g.yo ba here names the buddha of the eastern maṇḍala, Akṣobhya ("the Unshakable"), not the wrathful deity Acala; the context (the four buddhas with Ratnasaṃbhava, Amitāyus and Amoghasiddhi) makes this certain. Many epithets in the hymn of a hundred and eight names are given in English where they are descriptive rather than established deity names. The last line of the piece is terse; it appears to mean that the disciple's request ("Bless me," and so forth) is followed by the reception of the master's command. In the passage on Dharma-minded beings, the Tibetan as transmitted reads "even the accomplishment of becoming all the tathāgatas is difficult" (dka' ba), which contradicts the a fortiori "what need to speak of other accomplishments?" that follows and the parallel statement to the disciple later in the passage; it has been translated as "is not difficult," taking a negative particle to have dropped out. The vajra master's actions in the opening verses are addressed in humble verb forms, as speech directed to the Blessed One. "What is perilous" renders mi bde ba, which here appears to stand for wrongful or hazardous conduct whose non-avoidance leads to hell; the sense is not wholly certain. The wrathful binding of the mudrā before the disciple speaks words of the Great Vehicle is described tersely, and the exact gesture is unclear. The verse line rendered "for as long as one wishes oneself to rise" reads བདག་ཉིད་བདག་པ, which is unclear as written; the sense is taken from the parallel verses, which speak of soaring for as long as one wishes. In the section on the secret mudrās, "it" that is made to enter the opening of a woman or a man is unspecified in the Tibetan; it is presumably the mudrā or the practitioner's mind. The phrase rendered "vajra pride" (rdo rje bsgyings pa) reflects the proud posture of Vajrasattva holding the vajra. In the line "If the sattva vajra is accomplished," the Tibetan reads ma grub, "not accomplished," which makes no sense beside the parallel lines; it is taken as a slip for grub. The descriptions of the samaya mudrās are terse hand instructions, often without naming the mudrā; they are rendered literally, following the order of the sixteen bodhisattvas and the offering and gatekeeper goddesses of the Vajradhātu maṇḍala. The verse listing the five buddha mudrās names the fourth only as gdon mi za ba, "the Unfailing One," taken here as Amoghasiddhi; elsewhere in this passage the same phrase is the adverb "without fail." The terse descriptions of the action mudrās follow the order of the sixteen bodhisattvas and the offering and gatekeeper goddesses, and are rendered literally; several of their hand positions are obscure without a commentary. The phrase rendered "if one wishes to give oneself up" (bdag gtong bar 'dod na) is terse; it may refer to the practitioner wishing to abandon the practice or the vow. The phrase "with the hands joined side by side" renders lag pa gshibs pas, followed by a word (bsad ba) that is unclear as written; the sense of performing the vajra dance is taken from context. The sixteen dhāraṇī goddesses are named in Tibetan by epithets. Where the Sanskrit name is well established it is given (Samantabhadrā, Tathāgatāṅkuśī, Ratnottamā, Ratnolkā, Dhvajāgrakeyūrā, Hāsavatī, Samantacakrā, Siddhottarā, Sarvarakṣā); the others (dga' ba chags ma, legs ldan ma, rdo rje padma, kun 'dzin ma, steng du zlos ma, mdangs slar 'phrog ma, gzungs kyi phyag rgya) are translated by meaning, since their Sanskrit forms cannot be fixed with confidence. Several lines of the closing verses describing the emblems are terse and are rendered literally. In the line "Extending the thumbs, cover the vajra," the text reads mtho bo, which makes no sense here; it is taken as a slip for mthe bo, "thumb." The female figures of the body mudrā verses (the vajra lady, the hook lady, the lady of vajra joy) are rendered descriptively rather than given reconstructed Sanskrit names. The line rendered "The eternal lord is sent forth" reads btang nga re, an unusual verbal ending; the sense of this closing line of the verse is uncertain. In the first verse, the word read here as "form" of the moon (dpas) is unclear; the line evidently refers to visualizing oneself as, or within, the moon disk. The verses listing the sixteen samaya mudrā placements are extremely terse, and the rendering of several of these lines is tentative. The sixteen offering goddesses are given here only by descriptive Tibetan epithets, which are rendered in English rather than with reconstructed Sanskrit names. The epithet rendered "the lady who invites" reads spyan 'dren me, evidently a slip for spyan 'dren ma. The concluding statement of the Vajrakarma section ends with the syllable sho where so is expected; it is read as the ordinary closing particle. The disciple is not named in the Tibetan of the final paragraph but is supplied from the context of the maṇḍala rite of entry; the speech to the disciple continues into the next piece. In the verse on the crossed fingers, the Tibetan reads dgos mchog, "supreme need"; this is taken as a slip for dngos mchog, "supreme accomplishment," in keeping with the Vajrasiddhi maṇḍala. The verse beginning "Having oneself made the sign stable" is terse, and its sense of sign (nimitta) and stūpa is rendered literally. In the hymn, rdo rje dgos, "vajra purpose," may be a slip for rdo rje dngos, "vajra accomplishment"; it is translated as written. The closing particle of the colophon is written sto for so. Vajrapāṇi's humble reply ma 'tshal lo is taken as a refusal, "I cannot," which his explanation that follows bears out. In Vajrapāṇi's command, the text reads da'i dam tshig; da'i is taken as a slip for nga'i, "my samaya." In Maheśvara's question, dbang phyug is rendered Īśvara, the Lord, by which he refers to himself. Maheśvara's words "You too, listen to his command and take up the samaya" are rendered as they stand; the Tibetan does not make clear whom he addresses or whose command is meant. The world system and the tathāgata into which the Great God is reborn, rendered in Tibetan as "Covered with Ash" and "Voice of the Lord of Ash," are given here under their Sanskrit names Bhasmacchannā and Bhasmeśvaranirghoṣa. Several of the new vajra names and some of the gods' names are translated into English because their Sanskrit forms could not be identified with confidence; these include Karṇika, the Youth of Sound, Earth-Crest, Great Light, and the women called the Slayer, the Eater and the Clothed One. "Vajra Deep Lady" renders rdo rje zab ma as written; it may be a slip for a name formed from za ba, "eater," to match the goddess's original name. "Heart-essence" renders སྙིང་རུས (literally "heart and bone"); the sense is that the spirits may consume the heart of those whose death is imminent, as the preceding mantra on the signs of death indicates. In the verse on the maṇḍala, བཞེས in "four archways" is read as བཞི, "four." Vajrapāṇi is addressed as "Blessed One" by the spirits, following the Tibetan. The figures drawn at the centers of the second, third and fourth maṇḍalas are named in the Tibetan as "Vajra Empowerment," "Vajra Host," and "Vajra Descent"; I could not identify their Sanskrit names with confidence and have translated them literally. Here རྡོ་རྗེ་ཁྲོ་བོ is used as a class term, "vajra wrathful ones," and as the epithet of Tirintiri, rather than as the proper name Vajracaṇḍa. In the verses on the secret wrathful mudrā, the four effects (ruin of life, being drawn into one's presence, bliss, suffering, protection) correspond to the ritual aims of the mantras listed after them; the verses are terse and the referents ("that one," "whoever") follow the Tibetan without supplying a subject. The phrase rendered "the stillness of slaying" (གསོད་སྙོམ) is obscure. In the list of the wrathful ones, the lines rendered "he acts as a māra" and "overpowers all dharmas" both read བདུད ("māra"), which is obscure in this context; the sense may be that these wrathful forms overpower the māras. The four syllables and the list of seed syllables after them are given exactly as written. "Colors" renders མཚོན, read as ཚོན (the maṇḍala pigments), since the verse follows the laying of the cords; མཚོན can also mean "emblems." The line "draw at the center a spear point with lips" (མདུང་རྩེ་མཆུར) is obscure. The colophon spelling རྫོགས་སྷོ is read as རྫོགས་སོ, "is complete." The verses on the mudrās are terse ritual instructions whose hand positions are often only alluded to; the rendering follows the Tibetan literally. In the responding dance, the Tibetan has an unclear "na pa" after "left," taken here as "left hand." "Mdzub me'i" is read as "mdzub mo'i" (fingertips), an evident slip. The mudrā verses are terse and allude to hand gestures without fully describing them; they are rendered literally. In the final verses, the line "lhan cig gyur pa cher bstan pa" is obscure; it is taken as one who shows himself allied with a being's enemy. Several verses are terse and obscure. The line "sna nas phra ba thang cig min" is taken as continuous subtle breathing through the nose. "Bu gnas" in the verse on drawing out the heart is read as "womb," which is uncertain; the line also lacks a syllable. The goddess generated from Vajrapāṇi's heart is given as plural, since the Tibetan does not mark number and the following actions describe a group of offering goddesses. "Gdon mi za bar" is the idiom "without doubt," not a reference to seizing spirits. Several homa ingredients are uncertain: "se ba'i ne ra ma can" is taken as thorny wild rose, "mi snang me tog" as a plant called the invisibility flower, and "nam mkha'i lcug ma" as the sky creeper (likely dodder, ākāśavallī). "Skye bo rengs" is literally "people become stiff" or transfixed; in the sequence of summoning, enamoring, binding and killing given by the mantras, it may stand for enthralling. "Rdo rje gtso" (the Vajra Lord) heads the four deities of the surrounding maṇḍalas and is not identified with confidence. In the verses on secret karma mudrās, "bu ga" (opening) is taken as the woman's secret organ, as the context indicates. In the verse on the secret mudrā of Vajra Hūṃ, "bu ga" (opening) is taken, as in the preceding passage, as the woman's secret organ, and "the finger of Victorious over the Three Worlds" as the corresponding mudrā; the language is deliberately veiled. "Mngon 'dus pa" in the closing verse is rendered "the very essence," taking it as the Great Vehicle in its condensed form. The world system ཐལ་བས་ཁེབས་པ ("Covered with Ashes") is rendered by meaning; it corresponds to Sanskrit Bhasmacchannā. The mudrā name ཟླ་བའི་ཞབས is rendered literally as "Moon Foot", since its Sanskrit form could not be identified with confidence. The sentence in which Vajrapāṇi "becomes rays of light" from the uṣṇīṣas and settles on his own crown is translated as the Tibetan stands; the syntax is compressed. The mantra oṃ vajraśūla is introduced here as the heart of the vidyā king རྡོ་རྗེ་ཁྲོ་བོ, rendered Vajracaṇḍa to match earlier pieces. Here རྡོ་རྗེ་ཁྲོ་བོ is used as the title of a class of deities ("the vajra wrathful ones," alongside the kings of vidyās, lords of hosts, messengers and servants), so it is translated as a class title rather than as the personal name Vajracaṇḍa used earlier. The gaṇapati རྡོ་རྗེ་གླང་སྣ is rendered by meaning, "Vajra Elephant Trunk," because its Sanskrit form could not be identified with confidence (perhaps Vajraśauṇḍa). The line ས་ལ་བར་དུ་བྲིས་པ is compressed, and "drawn on the ground in the middle" is a tentative reading. In the line གཡོན་པས་རྡོ་རྗེ་གཟུར་ནས་སོ, གཟུར makes no sense here and is taken as a slip for གཟུང ("held"), giving "holding the vajra with the left hand." The mudrā name མེ་ཏོག་བཞི་པ is rendered "the Fourfold Flower"; its exact Sanskrit name is uncertain. The phrase རས་ཀྱི་རྒྱུ་རས in the verse on the messengers is obscure; "the woven cloth" is a tentative rendering. The four lines beginning "Likewise, as the principal of the maṇḍala" appear twice in the Tibetan and are translated both times as they stand. The goddess སྒྲ་དབང is rendered by meaning, "the Mistress of Sound," since her Sanskrit name could not be identified with confidence. The text has ཕྱག་ན་རྡོ་རྗེ་སྤྱན where the sequence calls for Vajranetra (རྡོ་རྗེ་སྤྱན), and it is translated as Vajranetra. Several verses in this section are terse and their exact sense is uncertain, in particular the line གདུག་ལ་བྱམས་དང་སྐྱོ་བར་ནི in the oath ("or are cruel to the kind and bring them grief"), and the opening of the verse on the servants, མཆེ་བདག་པར་ཞུགས་པ ("having entered as the lord of fangs"). The statement that intercourse with women brought under power "will not become contrary to the Dharma" is given as the text states it, in the context of the tantra's transgressive ritual idiom. The Tibetan name of the wrathful goddess rendered Vajrāmṛtā reads "vajra not binding" (mi 'ching ba); this is evidently a slip for "vajra deathless" (mi 'chi ba, amṛta), which her mantra confirms. Several goddesses' Tibetan names are obscure or unidentifiable as written (rdo rje tsong tsong, rdo rje 'jums, rdo rje du sems); their names are given here as Vajramālā, Vajravalayā and Vajrakālī on the basis of the vocatives in their own mantras, and are tentative. "Vajra Tresses" (rdo rje ral ma) is a literal rendering of a name I could not identify with confidence. The line "Lay out all the maṇḍalas with cord" appears twice in the Tibetan, probably by dittography; both are kept. The rendering "great forefather" translates mes po chen po, an epithet (pitāmaha) usually belonging to Brahmā. The line rendered "Great dread, equal to the great" (dogs pa chen po che dang mtshungs) is obscure as written and is translated literally. "The vajra swagger" (rdo rje 'gying) may be a posture or the name of a deity; it is rendered literally. The line rendered "its corners opening into gates" (gru ni sgo gdod cing) is obscure as written. "Bhṛkuṭī" renders khro gnyer, "frown," identified from the padma bhṛkuṭī mantra that follows in the next piece. "The goddess of speech herself" renders sgra dang lha mo bdag nyid, most likely Sarasvatī. "The Glory of the Lotus" (padma dpal) probably refers to the goddess Śrī (Lakṣmī), who dwells in the lotus. The line rendered "With the whole assembly and the rest" (kun du tshogs sogs) is obscure as written. The first line reads kha ("mouth"), evidently a slip for mkha' ("sky"), as in the parallel line that follows; it is translated as "sky." Names of the deities in the list of actions (the Liberated One, the Fearless One, Pāṇḍaravāsinī for "White-Clad," Nīlakaṇṭha for "Blue-Throat," Pracaṇḍa for "Very Fierce") are given as identified from the Tibetan; "the Lord of Certainty" (nges pa'i dbang phyug) is a literal rendering. The phrase gdon mi za bar is the idiom "without doubt," not a reference to seizing spirits. The closing word of the chapter colophon is printed རྫོགས་སྷོ, evidently a slip for རྫོགས་སོ ("is complete"), and is translated so. "Crossing over with Vajra Force" seems to describe stepping into each successive maṇḍala using the Vajra Force (Vajravega) gait or mudrā. The names given to some of the mudrās in the first verses ("she of the lotus," "the one who shows") are translated literally, because the Tibetan does not plainly give them as fixed titles. "Having brought about the descent" translates ཕབ་ནས, taken here as the descent of wisdom into the disciple (āveśa), which is called for by the mantras and mudrās of descent just before; it could also mean letting the flower fall. In the verse on destroying, "a pure lotus" renders གཙང་ as written, though the mantras that follow (ccheda, "cut") suggest the lotus is to be broken. "The great Lord of Various Wisdoms" is a literal rendering, because a fixed Sanskrit name could not be identified with confidence. In the second line, ས་སྤྲད is taken as "touch the ground"; the line is terse and may instead describe the index fingers simply being joined. "Samantāvalokiteśvara" renders འཕགས་པ་ཀུན་དུ་སྤྱན་རས་གཟིགས་ཀྱི་དབང་པོ, where ཀུན་དུ ("in every direction") stands before the usual name. The doubled syllable in ལྟག་པར་རབ་ཏུ་བརྐྱང་བར་བར་བྱ is taken as a copyist's repetition. "A form made of sound" renders སྒྲ་ཡི་གཟུགས literally; the sense of this line is unclear. In the verses on bringing others under one's power, གདོན་མི་ཟ་བར is the idiom "without fail," not a reference to seizing spirits. "Making love with" translates རྩེ་བྱེད, literally "playing with," which in this ritual context of the secret mudrās refers to sexual union. The final word of the colophon, རྫོགས་སྟོ, is evidently a slip for རྫོགས་སོ ("is complete"). The lines "Wherever it strikes, it performs the works of increase, of the earth, and the rest" are terse and their exact ritual sense is uncertain. "Viśvadhara" renders སྣ་ཚོགས་འཆང, "Holder of the Manifold", identified with reasonable but not complete confidence. The colophon line praises Vajrasattva, Vajraratna and Vajrakarma as the Tibetan has it, although the section concerns the Dharma family. The line "Greatness itself, the tree, lord of jewels" renders an obscure Tibetan line, probably alluding to the wish-fulfilling tree. "Acts that cared for their wealth" renders ནོར་བསྐྱང་བ, which may be a slip for ནོར་བསྐྱེད་པ, "producing wealth". "Vajra Force" is kept as the name already used earlier in the text. "Blessed with jewels" reads རིན་པོ་ཆེ་ས་བྱིན་གྱིས་བརླབས་པ as རིན་པོ་ཆེས, taking the detached ས as the instrumental; it could also mean "blessed with jewels and earth". "The samaya should be made twofold" renders the terse དམ་ཚིག་གཉིས་སུ་བྱའོ, whose exact ritual sense is uncertain. The Full Realization of the Great Vehicle (ཐེག་པ་ཆེན་པོ་མངོན་པར་འདུས་པ) appears to name a recitation text or formula. The verses on hand gestures are terse, and some finger positions are rendered literally where the exact configuration is uncertain. Jewel Dharma appears twice in the list of mudrā actions, as the Tibetan has it. The colophon here names the King of Ritual Manuals of the Karma Samaya, although the section concerns the Gem family; it is translated as it stands. The line "Applying them to those very ones" renders the terse དེ་དག་ཉིད་ལ་རྣམ་པར་བརྟགས, whose exact sense in the gesture is uncertain. The Tibetan rdo rje rin chen serves both as the name of the deity Vajraratna and as the emblem he holds, the vajra jewel; it is rendered "Vajraratna" where a samādhi is named and "vajra jewel" where the object is drawn, worn or meditated upon. In the fourth verse "las kyis" is read as "las kyi", the karma mudrā, matching the series of great, samaya, Dharma and karma mudrās and their four mantras. The closing verses describe the hand gestures of the deities of the gem family, those of the sun, banner and laughter corresponding to Vajratejas, Vajraketu and Vajrahāsa. The colophon's closing particle is written sho, evidently a slip for so, the ordinary sentence ending; it does not affect the sense. The first supplication, rin chen don byin, could also be read "grant precious aims" or "grant jewels and wealth"; the series of mantras (all aims, all jewels, Dharma, action) supports taking don as aims or wealth. In the third supplication the text reads tshes bdag stsol, which makes no sense here; it is taken as chos bdag stsol, "grant me the Dharma," a similar-sounding reading that fits the series of accomplishment, empowerment, Dharma and action. The name of the karma maṇḍala, rin chen dgos pa, is rendered "the Jewel Purpose"; dgos pa may also mean "need" or "requisite." The colophon's closing particle sho is evidently a slip for so. "The Blessed One, all the tathāgatas" renders the Tibetan apposition literally; it may refer to the Buddha as the embodiment of all tathāgatas. The repeated line "if one does not wish it" means that a practitioner who does not seek buddhahood in this life gains the ordinary accomplishments described before. The colophon's closing particle sho is evidently a slip for so. The line ma rungs bdud byas dngos grub 'thob in the vajra family's Dharma verse is terse; it is taken to mean that one gains accomplishment by acting against vicious māras, though it could also be read as treating the vicious as māras to be subdued. "Radiant" renders gzi can, here an epithet of the accomplished practitioner, not the nāga king's name. "The Buddha Abhiṣeka" is kept for consistency with earlier pieces; in this verse the phrase may simply mean "the consecration of the buddhas," the gift-giving of the jewel family being likened to it. The verse on the vajra family's wisdom accomplishment has only three lines in the Tibetan. "The Great Awakening Mind" (Tibetan byang chub kyi sems chen po, perhaps Mahābodhicitta) and "Offering to the Buddhas" (sangs rgyas mchod pa) are rendered literally as epithets of the speaking deity, since a standard Sanskrit form could not be confirmed. "The mudrā of accomplishing all aims" may refer to Sarvārthasiddhi. The phrase "above and below" (ltag 'og) in the higher-knowledge verses is obscure; it seems to mean the proper sequence of the ritual. In the lotus-family samaya verse, "in no way will it come undone" reads grol mi 'gyur as the mudrā or samaya not being loosened; it could also be read "they will not be liberated at all." "Day by day" renders the unusual nyi rim du; the sense of a gradual or staged accomplishment is likely, but the exact reading is uncertain. The epithet kun du spyan ras gzigs kyi dbang po is rendered "Avalokiteśvara, who looks on all" (possibly Samantāvalokiteśvara). The first verse belongs to the Tathāgata family section announced at the end of the preceding piece.
